## U2（33 页）

1 
 
U2 Conquering poverty: When aspirations meet action 
 
Unit overview 
Poverty alleviation, a core UN Sustainable Development Goal, is not only a global 
endeavor crucial for improving living standards and promoting social equity, but also a 
key to unlocking economic potential and enhancing well-being worldwide. This unit 
offers a range of perspectives on poverty alleviation, highlighting both China’s 
remarkable achievements and global efforts. In Understanding China, an American 
journalist’s observations and the “Cliff Village” case illustrate the Chinese 
government’s commitment to and remarkable achievements in poverty alleviation and 
rural revitalization. Exploring the world examines the developing countries’ endeavors 
to alleviate poverty. These diverse strategies can help Ss appreciate how each society 
tailors its own distinctive path to poverty alleviation. Additionally, in Communicating 
with the world, Ss are asked to role-play an interview showcasing China’s poverty 
alleviation strategies and achievements as well as its rural revitalization endeavors, 
which can help them deepen their understanding of China’s poverty alleviation 
experience and develop a global perspective. 
 
思政元素清单 
Understanding China — Viewing 
• 
精准施策，因地制宜制定脱贫方案。 
• 
创新驱动发展，利用多种创新方式助力脱贫攻坚。 
• 
脱贫不是终点，而是新征程的起点，持续推动乡村振兴等新发展。 
Understanding China — Reading 
• 
以人民为中心，致力于消除贫困，提升人民生活质量。 
• 
基础设施建设助力脱贫。 
• 
产业发展带动脱贫。 
• 
脱贫后向乡村振兴迈进，追求更高质量发展和人民幸福。 
 
 
校园资料仓

2 
 
Exploring the world — Reading  
• 
尊重各国主权，鼓励各国根据自身国情探索适合的脱贫道路。 
• 
为建设没有贫困、普遍繁荣的人类命运共同体而共同努力。 
• 
不同国家脱贫经验可相互学习借鉴，促进国际间在脱贫领域的交流与合作。 
 
单元开篇 
Quote  
……脱贫摘帽不是终点，而是新生活、新奋斗的起点。 
——2021 年2 月25 日，习近平在全国脱贫攻坚总结表彰大会上的讲话  
 
Key facts about China 
The key facts presented in this unit are linked to the efforts and achievements of 
poverty alleviation and rural revitalization in China, ranging from population-centered 
poverty reduction to infrastructure-driven development and employment-based 
sustainability. These facts not only demonstrate the scale of China’s achievements but 
also provide insights into the effective strategies employed.   
Key fact 1: This fact represents the core of poverty alleviation, showing that China has 
fulfilled its promise of eliminating extreme poverty for a large rural population. It 
reflects the essence of people-centered development in poverty alleviation and rural 
revitalization, aiming at improving people’s living standards and ensuring everyone’s 
well-being. 
Key fact 2: This fact emphasizes infrastructure development, a crucial factor in poverty 
alleviation and rural revitalization. Infrastructure promotes economic development, 
facilitates communication and trade, and provides convenience for people in rural areas, 
enabling them to better integrate into the modernized world. 
Key fact 3: This fact focuses on the connection between employment and poverty 
alleviation. Stable employment is the key to consolidating poverty alleviation 
achievements and promoting rural revitalization. It ensures that those who have escaped 
poverty can have a permanent income and achieve long-term development, contributing 
to long-term prosperity in rural areas. 
*T can guide Ss to access the digital textbook for the latest information and data 
on Ucampus. 
校园资料仓

3 
 
Teaching suggestions 
The teaching aim of this unit’s Lead-in section is to immerse Ss in China’s poverty 
alleviation and rural revitalization efforts. The focus is on helping them 
comprehensively understand targeted poverty alleviation strategies and the transition to 
rural revitalization. Through this, students will not only recognize China’s remarkable 
poverty-reduction achievements but also internalize the people-centered values behind 
these efforts. The suggested teaching steps are as follows: 
Step 1: Begin the class by guiding students to browse the captioned pictures in this unit 
and encouraging them to share some key words related to China’s poverty alleviation. 
Guide Ss to analyze the quote of the unit and encourage them to briefly discuss the 
terms “finish line” and “starting point,” and share their understanding of them. Help 
them recognize that while poverty alleviation marks the finish line of one journey, it 
also serves as the starting point for China’s commitment to rural revitalization. 
Step 2: Have Ss review the key facts about China’s poverty alleviation and rural 
revitalization efforts. Organize them into small groups to discuss which dimension is 
reflected in each fact. 
Step 3: Encourage Ss to brainstorm additional measures and achievements related to 
poverty alleviation and rural revitalization beyond the given facts. Guide them to 
consider initiatives such as relocation projects, microfinancing, and East-West paring-
up programs.  
Step 4: Guide Ss through Scenario and help them identify the gap between their existing 
knowledge of China’s poverty alleviation and rural revitalization efforts and the 
knowledge they need to successfully complete the task outlined in Scenario. 
Step 5: Outline the unit’s learning objectives to help Ss stay focused and actively 
engage with the content in a meaningful and effective way.  
 
 
校园资料仓

4 
 
Understanding China 
The video in this section offers an overview of China’s poverty alleviation 
endeavors, highlighting elements like targeted policies, innovation-led development, 
and the seamless transition from poverty alleviation to rural revitalization. The text, 
using the real-world example of “Cliff Village,” demonstrates the practical application 
of these concepts. It shows how locally customized poverty alleviation plans, such as 
infrastructure construction, relocation, and industrial development, are carried out. The 
two materials are intricately linked, with the “Cliff Village” case in the second material 
vividly exemplifying the principles set forth in the first. 
In the Understanding China section, the main teaching objective is to enable Ss to 
comprehend China’s poverty alleviation and rural revitalization strategies and 
accomplishments. The challenge lies in equipping Ss with effective intercultural 
communication skills to convey these efforts and achievements to global audiences. 
These materials vividly illustrate the core values and principles underlying China’s 
poverty alleviation and rural revitalization. Targeted policies and locally-tailored plans 
mirror the “people-centered development” concept. The ongoing progression from 
poverty alleviation to rural revitalization is in line with the principle of sustainable 
development. Such efforts manifest China’s dedication to enhancing people’s 
livelihoods and constructing a more prosperous future. 
 
Viewing 
Before viewing 
Sample answers 
1 
In my hometown, which used to be a poverty-stricken area, the most obvious 
change is in infrastructure. In the past, the roads were narrow and bumpy. It was 
extremely difficult for villagers to transport agricultural products to the market. 
However, the initiatives for poverty alleviation included the construction of wide and 
smooth tarmac roads, connecting every village. Now, trucks can easily drive in and out, 
and the transportation of agricultural products has become much easier. 
Moreover, in terms of industry, many poverty-stricken areas have developed 
locally-based industries under the guidance of poverty alleviation policies. For 
example, some places have started to grow high-value cash crops like tea and fruits. 
Special technical teams were sent to teach farmers how to plant and manage these crops. 
校园资料仓

5 
 
As a result, farmers’ incomes have increased significantly, enabling them to get out of 
poverty successfully. 
 
Scripts 
I came to China in 2006, and have since traveled in every provincial-level region 
on the Chinese mainland to experience and contribute to China’s poverty alleviation 
miracle and overall development. I’ve not only chronicled these journeys in hundreds 
of China Daily stories and videos, but have encapsulated these unexpected adventures 
in my book. I’ve often been surprised by the unconventional solutions I’ve encountered 
throughout the country.  
President Xi Jinping proposed the targeted poverty alleviation strategy. From the 
deserts of Inner Mongolia to the tundra of Xizang, from the forests of Guizhou to the 
prairies of Hebei, Chinese people have developed unexpected innovations to overcome 
the often extreme and complicated causes of poverty throughout this vast nation. 
In the Daliang Mountains of Sichuan Province, I visited Abuluoha, the last village 
on the Chinese mainland to connect to the outside world by road. Building the road 
required using an Mi-26 helicopter to fly in excavators and loaders. After its 
completion, travel times to and from the village were greatly reduced. For me, it was 
incredible to see first-hand how the village finally ended its isolation. In Desheng 
Village, on the parched outskirts of Zhangjiakou, Hebei Province, I’ve seen how the 
disadvantages brought about by drought have been turned into advantages through the 
use of solar power. Its solar farm has turned sunshine into money and the villagers are 
gaining new affluence. In Ganzhou, Jiangxi Province, I joined a livestream in a farmer’s 
kiwifruit orchard. Many people like him are using livestreams and e-commerce to sell 
products around the country, rapidly accelerating poverty alleviation in such 
communities. Not only have incomes substantially increased, but other areas of quality 
of life have also improved, such as healthcare, education, housing, and drinking water. 
Nearly 100 million impoverished rural residents were lifted out of poverty in 
nearly a decade up to the end of 2020. And rural residents’ per capita disposable income 
increased to $1,951 (12,588 yuan) in previously impoverished areas in 2020. Shaking 
off poverty is not the finish line, but the starting point for new life and new endeavors. 
From achieving a complete victory in the fight against poverty to advancing rural 
revitalization, across the board, China is creating a better future in the new era.  
 
 
校园资料仓

6 
 
Notes 
1 
In 2013, President Xi Jinping proposed “targeted poverty alleviation” for the first 
time during a visit to Shibadong Village in Hunan Province. In its poverty 
elimination effort, China launched a series of guidelines and measures to increase 
efficiency, summarized as accomplishing “Targeted Efforts in Six Areas,” taking 
“Five Measures for Poverty Eradication,” and addressing “Five Questions in 
Poverty Alleviation.”  
⚫ 
The “Targeted Efforts in Six Areas” are efforts to accurately identify the poor 
people, arrange targeted programs, utilize capital efficiently, take household-
based measures, dispatch first Party secretaries according to the conditions in 
the village, and achieve the set goals. 
⚫ 
The “Five Measures for Poverty Eradication” include: boosting the economy 
to provide more job opportunities, relocating poor people from inhospitable 
areas, compensating the economic losses associated with reducing ecological 
damage, improving education in impoverished areas, and providing 
subsistence allowances for those unable to shake off poverty through their own 
efforts alone.  
⚫ 
The “Five Questions in Poverty Alleviation” ask: who should help, who 
should be helped, how to help, how to assess whether someone has emerged 
from poverty, and how to ensure those people stay free from poverty. 
2 
Abuluoha (阿布洛哈村) is a village encompassed by mountains and cliffs, located 
in a river valley about 60 kilometers from Butuo County in southwest China’s 
Sichuan Province. With a population of merely 253 people, most of whom are of 
the Yi ethnic group, Abuluoha had long suffered from poverty and poor transport 
infrastructure due to the rugged mountainous terrain. 
Driven by nationwide anti-poverty efforts, great changes have taken place in 
Abuluoha within years. Kids started to receive primary education when the 
village’s very first school was established in 2005. The villagers gained access to 
safe drinking water and electricity supply in 2007 and 2010, respectively. By 2019, 
the village had been provided with full 4G network coverage. 
On June 29, 2020, villagers moved out of thatch houses into concrete ones at a 
poverty-relief relocation site. The next day, the launch of a 3.8-kilometer road 
along cliffs and ravines marked a new dawn in the village’s history. 
3 
About 10 kilometers northeast of downtown Zhangbei County in northern Hebei 
Province lies Desheng Village (德胜村). In Chinese, “Desheng” rhymes with the 
word “success.” But for many years, the villagers lived in poverty. 
 
校园资料仓

7 
 
Due to its barren land and harsh climate, the area had unstable and usually poor 
agricultural production. In 2013, more than half of the 413 households in the 
village lived in poverty. In view of its conditions and resources, Desheng Village 
decided to develop mini potato farming, photovoltaic power generation, and rural 
tourism. The village went through transformative changes.  
*T can guide Ss to study the multimodal annotations in the digital textbook on 
Ucampus.  
 
Language points 
1 I came to China in 2006, and have since traveled in every provincial-level 
region on the Chinese mainland to experience and contribute to China’s 
poverty alleviation miracle and overall development. (Para. 1) 
alleviation: n. [U] the act of making sth. bad, such as pain or problems less severe 缓
解 
Access to clean water has played a crucial role in the alleviation of health issues in 
many remote areas. 
Note: Expressions with Chinese characteristics related to poverty alleviation include 
“targeted poverty alleviation (精准扶贫),” “poverty alleviation through business 
development (产业扶贫),” “poverty alleviation by developing the eco-economy (生态
扶贫),” “battle against extreme poverty (脱贫攻坚战),” etc. 
2 From the deserts of Inner Mongolia to the tundra of Xizang, from the forests 
of Guizhou to the prairies of Hebei, Chinese people have developed unexpected 
innovations to overcome the often extreme and complicated causes of poverty 
throughout this vast nation. (Para.2) 
Note: The sentence begins with a parallel structure “From ... to ..., from ... to ...” which 
emphasizes the wide geographical range within China. This highlights how different 
regions have implemented tailored poverty alleviation measures based on their unique 
circumstances. 
3 Building the road required using an Mi-26 helicopter to fly in excavators and 
loaders. (Para. 3) 
fly in: transport (goods, people, etc.) to a place by air 运送（货物、人员等）至某地 
The emergency team was flown in to the disaster-stricken area along with essential 
supplies to aid the victims. 
 
校园资料仓

8 
 
4 Not only have incomes substantially increased, but other areas of quality of life 
have also improved, such as healthcare, education, housing, and drinking 
water. (Para. 3) 
Note: This sentence employs the “not only ... but also ...” structure. When “not only” 
is placed at the beginning of the sentence and is followed by a verb phrase, the sentence 
must use partial inversion, as seen in “Not only have incomes substantially increased.” 
This is a common emphatic structure in English. 
5 From achieving a complete victory in the fight against poverty to advancing 
rural revitalization, across the board, China is creating a better future in the 
new era. (Para. 4) 
across the board: universally 全面地；包括一切地；涉及全体地 
The living conditions of rural residents have been improved across the board. 
Note: When connected with hyphens, “across-the-board” is used as an adjective, for 
example, The new across-the-board policy aims to promote economic development in 
all regions. 
 
Viewing and understanding  
Reference answers 
2 
1; 2; 4; 5; 7; 8 
 
Intercultural communication skill 
Teaching suggestions 
The teaching objective of this intercultural communication skill section is to 
enhance Ss’ ability to effectively utilize personal experiences to connect with and 
engage diverse audiences. The key focus lies in guiding Ss to apply these learned 
techniques in their own presentations, while also being aware of potential pitfalls like 
self-centeredness and irrelevance. T might find these teaching steps helpful: 
⚫ 
Guide Ss to analyze the narrator’s personal experiences in the text. Point out how 
the narrator describes his travels across different regions in China, e.g., “I came to 
China in 2006, and have since traveled in every provincial-level region on the 
Chinese mainland.” Ask Ss why this kind of broad-scope personal experience 
sharing is impactful. 
 
校园资料仓

9 
 
⚫ 
Discuss the specific expressions the narrator uses to convey his feelings, such as 
“I’ve often been surprised by ...” and “it was incredible to see ...” Have Ss consider 
how these expressions draw the reader in and make the story more engaging. 
⚫ 
Emphasize the importance of reflection as shown in the text. The narrator reflects 
on the impact of poverty alleviation measures, like in the cases of Abuluoha, 
Desheng Village, and Ganzhou. Ask Ss to identify these reflection points and 
explain how they add depth to the story. 
⚫ 
Have Ss present their stories in class. After each presentation, ask the rest of the 
class to provide feedback on how well the personal experience made the story 
appealing and how to improve the story. 
⚫ 
Warn Ss about potential pitfalls when sharing personal experiences in an 
intercultural context. For example, they should avoid being overly self-centered in 
their stories and make sure their experiences are relevant to the topic and the 
audience. 
* Remind Ss to access the video micro-lecture in the digital textbook to learn more 
about the skill on Ucampus.  
 
Sample answers 
3 
In my hometown, the impact of China’s poverty alleviation efforts can be seen 
everywhere. A few years ago, when I visited my grandparents during my summer 
vacation, I noticed that the roads were in a lamentable state – narrow, dirt tracks filled 
with potholes. Accompanying my grandfather to sell his home-grown vegetables at the 
local market was a back-breaking task. We had to load the produce onto an old, rickety 
cart and push it for miles. The rough terrain damaged many of the vegetables, and I felt 
a deep sense of frustration and worry, both for the wasted effort and the meager income 
we might earn. Fast-forward to last year’s visit, and I was greeted with a completely 
different sight. Wide tarmac roads now wind through the village, allowing trucks to 
come and go with ease. I witnessed villagers loading fresh fruits and vegetables onto 
trucks bound for cities. The local government had helped build greenhouses and 
provided training in modern farming techniques. This transformation filled me with a 
profound sense of excitement and hope. I could see the villagers’ happiness and 
enthusiasm, and my grandfather, once so burdened by financial stress, now beamed 
with pride as he told me their income had tripled in just a couple of years. 
 
 
校园资料仓

10 
 
Reading  
Text: From vine ladder to steel ladder 
Background information 
China has been committed to poverty alleviation for decades. After the founding 
of the People’s Republic of China, a land reform was rolled out across the country to 
abolish the feudal land system that had been in place for over 2,000 years. This action 
removed the major institutional obstacle to eliminating poverty. Subsequently, the 
reform and opening-up policy further accelerated socioeconomic development, giving 
a vigorous boost to poverty alleviation and resulting in a sharp decline in the number 
of impoverished people. As China marched into the new era, the 18th CPC National 
Congress in 2012 set the goal of building a moderately prosperous society in all 
respects. When launching the new-era poverty alleviation campaign, President Xi 
declared, “Not a single poverty-stricken area or individual would be left behind.” In 
2013, during his inspection tour to Shibadong Village, President Xi proposed for the 
first time the concept of “giving differentiated guidance for targeted poverty alleviation 
in line with local conditions by seeking truth from facts”. In 2015, at the National 
Conference on Development-driven Poverty Alleviation, he sounded the bugle call to 
battle, “We should be determined, dedicate ourselves, and work hard towards the goal.” 
Atulie’er, or “Cliff Village” accelerated its poverty-alleviation initiatives, becoming a 
microcosm of China’s relentless efforts in the battle against extreme poverty. By 2020, 
China had completed the arduous task of eliminating extreme poverty, fulfilling the 
poverty-reduction target of the UN 2030 Agenda for Sustainable Development 10 years 
ahead of schedule.    
 
Notes 
Registered as living in poverty (建档立卡贫困户): Registered impoverished 
households are identified through a national registration process to accurately assess 
poverty, analyze causes, and address needs. These households benefit from various 
support policies, including living subsidies, poverty alleviation loans, skills training, 
and special projects such as education, medical aid, and microloans. These measures 
aim to enhance self-development, promote self-reliance, and help families ultimately 
escape poverty and achieve prosperity. 
* T can also guide Ss to study the multimodal annotations in the digital textbook 
on Ucampus. 
 
 
 
 
校园资料仓

11 
 
Language points  
1 With a sense of bittersweet farewell, he tidied the ashes from the firepit, swept 
the courtyard floor, and closed the gate. (Para. 1) 
Note: The term “bittersweet” employs the rhetorical device of oxymoron, which 
combines two contradictory terms to create a unique and thought-provoking effect. 
“Bitter” implies unpleasantness, and “sweet” connotes pleasantness. Here, 
“bittersweet” describes the farewell. The “bitter” aspect is his heavy heart when leaving 
a place that holds memories, perhaps a place where he had lived for a long time. The 
“sweet” part is the anticipation of a new beginning or the relief of moving on from 
something.  
Besides, the sentence uses a series of parallel verb phrases “tidied,” “swept,” and 
“closed” to describe a sequence of actions. This structure further emphasizes his mixed 
emotions, as each action seems to symbolize a step in the process of letting go. 
2 West of the fertile plains of Sichuan lie high mountains, where some of the most 
impoverished villages are nestled in Liangshan Yi Autonomous Prefecture. 
(Para. 2)  
Meaning: To the west of Sichuan’s fertile plains, there are towering mountains, and 
they are home to some of the most impoverished villages in Liangshan Yi Autonomous 
Prefecture. 
Note: The sentence contains a subject-verb inversion structure. This type of inversion 
is common when a prepositional phrase is placed at the beginning of a sentence, 
especially in literary or descriptive writing. It draws attention to the location (“West of 
the fertile plains of Sichuan”) and creates a more formal or poetic tone. 
3 Poverty has long cast a shadow over this mountainous region, with the vine 
ladder standing in the way of a better life for the villagers. (Para. 3) 
cast a shadow over sth.: make a situation seem less hopeful and more likely to end 
badly 给…蒙上阴影 
The economic crisis cast a shadow over the future of the company. 
stand in the way of sth.: be an obstacle or hindrance that prevents sb. or sth. from 
progressing, achieving a goal, or happening as it should 阻碍；妨碍 
Fear of failure should never stand in the way of pursuing your dreams and achieving 
your goals. 
 
 
校园资料仓

12 
 
4 The village also established a preschool, enabling children to receive free 
preschool education without having to make the trek down the mountain. 
(Para. 5) 
trek:  
n. a long and difficult journey, made esp. on foot as an adventure （尤指徒步的）艰
苦长途旅行 
We set off on another four-hour trek through the swamps. 
v. make a long and difficult trip, esp. on foot （尤指徒步的）艰苦长途跋涉  
They had to trek a few kilometers to the nearest store. 
Note: In this context, “make the trek” refers to the effort required for children in the 
village to travel down the mountain to access preschool education. 
5 As more tourists arrived, many villagers started running small stores and 
home stays. (Para. 6) 
Note: In the sentence, “run” means to organize or to be in charge of an activity, 
business, organization, or country. For example: “She moved to the countryside to run 
a hotel that offered guests a peaceful retreat.” 
6 It was recognized that lifting all the residents in “Cliff Village” out of poverty 
would require an unbearably high cost to further improve infrastructure, 
including transportation, communication, and electricity. After careful 
consideration, the local government decided that it might be best to relocate 
these impoverished households to apartments specifically built for them in the 
county and help them settle and rebuild their lives there. (Para. 7) 
Note: The sentence explains the rationale behind relocating the villagers, citing the 
prohibitively high cost of further improving their living conditions as the primary 
reason. It shows the local government’s rational decision-making process, emphasizing 
that the decision was not made arbitrarily but was the result of careful deliberation.  
7 When these impoverished households move, their cliff houses can be 
repurposed for tourism, and a large amount of land also becomes available, 
allowing those who remain to have more per capita land for use. (Para. 8) 
Meaning: Once these poor families relocate, their former houses can be transformed 
for tourism. Additionally, a significant expanse of land is set free. As a result, the people 
who stay in the village will have a larger share of the land per capita at their disposal. 
 
 
校园资料仓

13 
 
repurpose: vt. find a new use for sth.; use sth. for a different purpose than it was 
originally intended 重新利用；改变用途  
They repurposed the old barn into a cozy restaurant. 
per capita: a. (fml.) used to describe the average amount of sth. in a particular place, 
calculated according to the number of people who live there 人均的；平均每人的 
The per capita income in this city has been increasing steadily. 
8 The path of “Cliff Village” continues to broaden, mirroring the sweeping 
changes that resonate across China’s rural landscape. (Para. 11) 
Note: In the sentence, the “path” is not just a physical road but a symbol representing 
the development or progress of “Cliff Village.” It symbolizes the journey of the village 
toward betterment, such as economic growth, social development, and improvement in 
people’s well-being. By saying the path is “broadening,” it implies that the village is 
experiencing more opportunities and a wider range of possibilities for development. 
Also, the “path … continues to broaden,” gives the “path” a sense of agency as if it is 
an entity that is actively evolving. This can be seen as a subtle form of personification, 
making the description more vivid. 
9 These villages, each with their own tale to tell, contribute to a harmonious 
chorus, hailing China’s remarkable transformation and unwavering 
dedication not only to eradicating poverty, but also to fostering sustainable 
growth, revitalizing rural areas, and ensuring that the sweetest fruits of 
progress are enjoyed by all its people. (Para. 11) 
Note: “Each with their own tale to tell” gives the villages a human-like quality. Villages 
are inanimate objects, but saying they have “tales to tell” implies that they have 
experiences, stories, and a kind of voice. This personification makes the reader think of 
the villages not as a location but as a community of people, each with their individual 
stories to tell.  
The phrase “contribute to a harmonious chorus” is a metaphor. Here, each village’s 
poverty alleviation story is compared to an individual voice that comes together to form 
a “chorus.” Just as a chorus is a group of voices that blend together to create a beautiful 
and unified sound, the villages come together to form a collective tribute to China’s 
achievements.  
 
 
 
校园资料仓

Translation  
从藤梯到钢梯 
1 
2020 年5 月13 日清晨，51 岁的某色达体比平时起得要早。阳光倾洒在海拔
1500 多米的半山腰小院里。即将跟老房子告别，他喜忧参半。清理了火塘的
灰烬，扫净了院子的地面，关上了家门。他驻足片刻，最后看了一眼熟悉的
老房子，然后转身迈步前行。这一天，他将和妻子下山，开启充满希望和无
限可能的新生活。 
2 
在富饶的四川平原以西，崇山峻岭之间坐落着凉山彝族自治州的几个极其贫
困的村庄，阿土列尔村便是其中之一，这里是某色达体的家。这个村子更广
为人知的名字是“悬崖村”，得名于那条由藤条和木棍搭建而成的“天梯”。
过去，村民们必须攀爬这座天梯，花费两到三个小时才能上下山。攀爬藤梯
极其危险，但村民们别无选择。 
3 
贫困长期笼罩着这片山区，攀爬藤梯的出行方式阻碍了村民通往更好生活。
自2016 年以来，中国大地上实施了前所未有的扶贫行动，“悬崖村”的生
活因此发生了翻天覆地的变化。 
4 
2016 年，当地政府筹集了100 万元，用6000 多根钢管建造了一条1.5 米宽
的钢梯。钢梯让上下山变得更容易、更安全。养羊合作社养的400 多头山羊
和80 多头绵羊，不用发愁销路了。同年种下的脐橙树到2019 年开始为村民
带来显著的收入。在大棚里，村民们开始种植高利润的三七药材，还用上了
滴灌。他们的收入得到了大幅提升。 
5 
随着钢梯的建设，基站也顺利架设，为村子带来了4G 网络。村民们再也不
用“爬到山顶找信号”了。无人机可以在五分钟内将急需药品送到诊所，解
决了以往基础物资匮乏的问题，成为村民的生命线。此外，村里还建了一所
幼儿园，孩子们无须下山即可接受免费的学前教育。 
6 
通过互联网，一些村民甚至成了“网红”，他们的短视频获得了数百万次观
看。“悬崖村”的名气越来越大，2019 年吸引了近10 万名游客。随着游客
的增多，不少村民开起了小商店和民宿，这些生意显著增加了村民收入，成
为他们的主要经济来源。 
7 
尽管部分村民的生活得到了改善，到2019 年，村里仍有84 户建档立卡贫困
户。人们认识到，要让“悬崖村”的所有居民脱贫，需要进一步改善基础设
施，包括交通、通信和电力，但所需成本高昂，难以承受。经过慎重考虑，
当地政府决定在县城为这些贫困家庭修建专门的安置房，帮助他们在那里安
居乐业。 
8 
贫困户搬迁后，他们悬崖上的住宅可以被改造用于发展旅游业。搬迁后还能
腾出土地，继续留在村里的村民人均可用土地面积得到增加。在发展旅游业

15 
 
的同时，他们还可以进行更大规模的种植和养殖，进一步拓展收入来源。 
9 
正是在这样的背景下，某色达体和其他贫困村民收拾行李，从山上搬迁下来。
他们的行李十分简单，只有床上用品和衣物，因为政府已经确保新家的各项
设施一应俱全。在安置区周边，十多个现代农业产业园正在紧锣密鼓地开发
中，为搬迁至此的村民提供本地就业机会。当地政府还提供技能培训、劳务
输出服务和补贴，帮助他们快速适应在城镇的新生活。 
10 从藤梯到钢梯，再到楼梯，当地政府将继续整合资源，将住房建设、城镇化
推进、乡村旅游开发以及教育均衡发展紧密结合，力求确保无论是选择留下
的村民还是搬迁的居民，都能走向富裕之路。 
11 “悬崖村”的道路越走越宽，反映出中国广大乡村的巨变。这些村庄各自独
特的故事交织在一起，奏响了一曲和谐的乐章，彰显了中国在消除贫困、推
动可持续发展以及振兴乡村方面所展现出的非凡转型与坚定决心，确保发展
成果惠及全体人民。

The word “bittersweet” conveys that Mose Dati had mixed feelings. “Sweet” refers

He was sad to say goodbye to the familiar, yet also somewhat looking forward to

2 
After the implementation of the anti-poverty initiative in 2016, “Cliff Village” 
underwent significant changes, including the construction of a steel ladder, 
improved access to economic activities such as goat farming, navel orange 
cultivation, and sanqi herb farming. A 4G network was introduced, enabling better 
connectivity. Drones delivered essential medicines to the clinic and a preschool 
was established for free education. Tourism flourished, attracting nearly 100,000 
visitors in 2019, with villagers running small stores and home stays to boost 
income. Additionally, some villagers became Internet celebrities, further 
enhancing the village’s visibility and economic opportunities. These 
transformations greatly improved the villagers’ quality of life and opened new 
avenues for development. 
3 
 
⚫ 
The villagers, including Mose Dati, moved away from the mountain due to the 
local government’s decision to relocate impoverished households to 
apartments specifically built for them in the county. This decision was made 
because, if they had remained at their original location, further enhancing their 
living standards would have required an unbearably high cost, as it would have 
been necessary to improve the infrastructure.  
⚫ 
Moving away transformed their lives as they were provided with modern 
housing and everything needed for their new homes. Modern agricultural 
industrial parks offered local job opportunities. The government also provided 
skills training, labor export services, and subsidies to help the relocated 
villagers adapt to urban life quickly. This relocation allowed the villagers to 
rebuild their lives with fresh hope and greater possibilities. 
4 
In the text, “vine ladder” symbolizes the old, primitive, and dangerous living 
conditions in “Cliff Village.” It represents the difficult and arduous way of life the 
villagers had to endure. “Steel ladder” symbolizes the first step of improvement in 
the infrastructure, a sign that the situation was starting to get better. It shows that 
the government was taking action to make the village more accessible. “Stairways” 
symbolize further progress and development. They represent a more stable and 
comfortable living environment, indicating that villagers are moving toward a 
more prosperous future. 
 
Intercultural communication skill  
Teaching suggestions 
The teaching objective of this intercultural communication skill section is to 
enhance Ss’ ability to effectively give reasons when describing China’s poverty 
校园资料仓

17 
 
alleviation achievements in an intercultural context. The key focus lies in guiding Ss to 
understand the importance of justifying actions rather than merely stating facts. 
Additionally, Ss are taught to use simple and clear language, avoiding jargon, when 
presenting reasons to foreign audiences. T may find the teaching steps helpful: 
⚫ 
Begin the class by briefly revisiting the concept of giving reasons in intercultural 
communication. Emphasize that in the context of discussing China’s lesser-known 
and often misunderstood policies and measures, it’s not just about stating facts but 
also about justifying why certain actions were taken.  
⚫ 
Use simple examples from everyday life to further illustrate the concept. For 
instance, if you are trying to convince a friend from another culture to try a Chinese 
delicacy they may not be accustomed to, you don’t just say “It’s good,” but you 
also explain why. For example, “It has a unique blend of spices that balance sweet 
and savory, which gives it a distinct flavor.” 
⚫ 
Facilitate a group discussion on the topic: “How would you introduce the poverty 
alleviation measure of relocation implemented in China?” Guide Ss to closely read 
paragraphs seven to nine and encourage them to share their insights. 
⚫ 
Divide the class into small groups, and ask them to brainstorm other poverty 
alleviation measures implemented in China might be unfamiliar to international 
audiences. Group members take turns role-playing as villagers or local officials, 
explaining the reasons behind various poverty alleviation measures. 
⚫ 
Teach Ss that when giving reasons to foreign audiences, they need to use simple 
and clear language. Remind them to avoid using jargon and to focus on explaining 
the concept in an accessible way. 
* Remind Ss to access the video micro-lecture in the digital textbook to learn more 
about the skill on Ucampus. 
 
Sample answers 
Ecological compensation(生态补偿) is an innovative poverty-alleviation strategy 
that combines environmental conservation with income generation for residents in 
ecologically fragile areas. This approach encourages sustainable development by 
rewarding individuals and communities for protecting and restoring natural ecosystems, 
rather than exploiting them for short-term economic gain. For example, impoverished 
residents in ecologically fragile areas are hired as forest rangers to protect forests, 
prevent illegal logging, and monitor wildlife. This creates jobs while ensuring 
environmental protection. In Yunnan Province, the government employed local 
villagers, many of whom were from ethnic minority groups, as forest rangers. They 
校园资料仓

18 
 
were tasked with patrolling protected areas, planting trees, and reporting environmental 
violations. This program provided stable incomes for thousands of families while 
preserving biodiversity in the region.

3 
China is a key advocate of and contributor to global poverty alleviation efforts. 
China had completely eradicated absolute poverty by 2020, and reached the poverty 
reduction goal of the United Nations 2030 Agenda for Sustainable Development a full 
decade ahead of schedule. On this basis, China has established a five-year transition 
period to consolidate the achievements in poverty alleviation and fully advance rural 
revitalization. Through a series of policies and practices, China has explored a unique 
development path, contributing its wisdom and strength to global poverty reduction and 
rural development. This demonstrates China’s active commitment to building a global 
community of shared future that is free from poverty and pursues common prosperity. 
 
校园资料仓

19 
 
Critical thinking  
Sample answers 
1 
The “targeted poverty alleviation strategy” can be defined as a highly focused and 
customized approach to poverty reduction. It doesn’t adopt a one-size-fits-all method 
but tailors solutions according to the specific circumstances of different impoverished 
regions and individuals.   
Six key aspects ensure the effectiveness of poverty alleviation efforts. These 
include: 
⚫ 
Precise identification of poverty-stricken individuals: Ensuring accurate 
identification of those in need of assistance by carefully assessing their 
circumstances. 
⚫ 
Precise arrangement of projects: Designing and implementing projects 
tailored to the specific needs of impoverished regions and individuals. 
⚫ 
Precise use of funds: Allocating financial resources efficiently and 
transparently to maximize their impact. 
⚫ 
Precise measures for each household: Developing targeted solutions that 
address the unique challenges faced by each household. 
⚫ 
Precise assignment of personnel to villages: Deploying capable personnel to 
villages to oversee and guide poverty alleviation efforts. 
⚫ 
Precise evaluation of poverty alleviation results: Monitoring and evaluating 
the outcomes of poverty alleviation initiatives to ensure their effectiveness 
and sustainability. 
“Cliff Village” is an example of targeted poverty alleviation. It demonstrates some 
of the “Six Precisions” of targeted poverty alleviation by identifying 84 impoverished 
households in “Cliff Village” (precise identification) and implementing tailored 
projects, such as building a steel ladder and introducing navel orange farming (precise 
arrangement of projects). Funds were efficiently used to improve the infrastructure and 
develop agricultural parks near resettlement areas (precise use of funds).  
2 
One measure for rural revitalization is the development of rural cultural tourism. 
For example, in some villages in southern China, policies and measures are made to 
encourage the protection of the traditional architecture and cultural heritage. Tourism 
routes are built to attract tourists to experience local folk customs, traditional 
handicrafts, and unique cuisines. This contributes to long-term prosperity in rural areas 
in several ways. Firstly, it attracts external consumption, increasing the income of 
villagers who can run homestays, sell handicrafts, and provide catering services. 
校园资料仓

20 
 
Secondly, it promotes the preservation of local culture, which can enhance the villagers’ 
sense of identity and pride. Thirdly, it can attract young people to return to their 
hometowns, bringing new ideas and technologies, thus strengthening the local 
workforce. 
Another measure is the promotion of agricultural modernization. In some large-
scale agricultural regions, the introduction of advanced agricultural machinery and 
intelligent irrigation systems has significantly improved agricultural productivity. For 
example, in the plains of Northeast China, large-scale farms use drones for crop 
spraying and automated harvesters. This reduces labor costs, increases the yield and 
quality of agricultural products, and makes rural agriculture more competitive. It also 
promotes the development of related industries such as agricultural machinery 
maintenance and agricultural technology consulting, creating more job opportunities in 
rural areas and contributing to long-term economic growth. 
The establishment of rural cooperatives is also an important measure. In many 
rural areas, farmers form cooperatives to jointly purchase production materials, sell 
agricultural products, and share resources. For instance, in a village that specializes in 
fruit growing, the cooperative negotiates better prices with buyers, reduces the cost of 
production materials through bulk purchases, and provides technical training to 
members. This helps to increase farmers’ income, improve their bargaining power in 
the market, and promote the sustainable development of rural industries. 
 
 
校园资料仓

21 
 
Exploring the world  
Building upon the in-depth analysis of China’s poverty alleviation endeavors and 
remarkable achievements, the instructional focus now pivots toward exploring diverse 
global approaches to poverty alleviation. The text in this section spotlights how distinct 
developing nations, such as Mozambique, Bangladesh, and Brazil, grapple with 
poverty. Each country confronts unique challenges and has crafted bespoke solutions. 
The teaching focus is to help Ss identify and understand poverty alleviation 
initiatives and achievements in these countries. The difficulty lies in guiding Ss to 
critically analyze the underlying rationales behind each country’s approach to 
combating poverty. 
T should stress that eradicating poverty is a shared goal among nations worldwide 
and an urgent need that deeply resonates with the “Global South.” Active efforts should 
be made to promote South-South cooperation and global collaboration, supporting and 
assisting developing countries, especially the least developed nations, in their fight 
against poverty. Together, countries should join hands to build a global community of 
shared future that is free from poverty and pursues common development.  
 
Text: Global strides in poverty alleviation 
Notes 
1 
Mozambique has built several development corridors to promote regional 
integration, economic growth, and sustainable development in the country and its 
neighbors. These corridors are typically centered around key transportation routes, 
such as highways, railways, and ports, and are designed to connect resource-rich 
areas with domestic and international markets. Notable corridors in Mozambique 
include the Maputo development corridor, the Beira development corridor, and the 
Nacala development corridor. 
2 
Initiated in 1995, Brazil’s Family Allowance Program expanded rapidly, 
covering 98% of poor families in 5,562 municipalities by 2001, benefiting 8.2 
million children from 4.8 million families. Targeting families with a monthly per 
capita income below 60 Brazilian reais or below 120 reais for households with 
pregnant women, breastfeeding mothers, or children under 15, the program reached 
11 million families by 2006. It significantly advanced social equity, reduced 
poverty, and broke the intergenerational cycle of poverty by linking cash transfers 
to conditions like school attendance, vaccinations, and prenatal care. 
 
校园资料仓

22 
 
*T can guide Ss to study the multimodal annotations in the digital textbook on 
Ucampus.  
 
Language points 
1 The diverse manifestations and causes of impoverished conditions require 
solutions that are tailored to each country’s specific context. (Para. 1) 
Meaning: Poverty manifests differently across countries, requiring solutions adapted 
to each nation’s unique circumstances. 
tailor sth. to：make or change sth. esp. for a particular person or purpose 针对…而
做；针对…而修改 
The newly designed marketing plan is tailored to the company’s long-term development 
goals. 
2 Agricultural development is slow, leading to low and inconsistent crop yields 
which feed into food insecurity. (Para. 2) 
Meaning: The slow pace of agricultural development results in low and unstable crop 
production, leading to food insecurity. 
feed into sth.: have an effect on sth. or help to make it happen 影响 
The new policies feed into the company’s overall success by improving employee 
satisfaction and fostering a more innovative work environment. 
3 All of these measures have diversified the economy and built resilience to 
economic shocks. (Para.3) 
Meaning: These efforts have expanded economic diversity and strengthened the 
country’s ability to withstand economic shocks. 
resilience: [U] n. the ability of a person or thing to feel better quickly after an 
unpleasant situation, such as shock, injury, or illness (In an economic context, it refers 
to the ability to recover from economic downturns.) 恢复力；弹性  
The economy’s resilience was tested during the crisis. 
4 Together, these efforts have made a significant dent in poverty across central 
Mozambique. (Para. 3) 
make a dent in sth.: reduce the amount of sth. 减少；削弱 
Her diligent efforts to save money have finally made a dent in her student loan debt, 
bringing her closer to financial freedom. 
 
校园资料仓

23 
 
5 Consequently, one of the primary goals for poverty reduction there is to 
address the landless poor, ensuring that they have access to the resources and 
opportunities needed to improve their livelihood and living conditions. (Para. 
4) 
Meaning: As a result, a key objective in reducing poverty in South Asia is to address 
the needs of the poor who lack access to land by providing them with the necessary 
resources and opportunities to improve their quality of life and economic well-being. 
have access to: be able to use or obtain sth. 拥有对…的使用权 
Having access to up-to-date financial information is important to our success. 
6 Borrowers repay these loans through small, regular payments, often within a 
supportive group structure, which fosters accountability and community 
solidarity. (Para.5) 
Meaning: People who have borrowed money (borrowers) pay back the loans they have 
received by making small payments at regular intervals. These payments are often made 
within a group setting that provides support. This group structure helps to create a sense 
of responsibility (accountability) among the borrowers and also strengthens the unity 
and togetherness (solidarity) within the community. 
Note: In Bangladesh’s microfinance model, borrowers are usually organized into 
groups, with group members providing mutual guarantees and sharing joint 
responsibility for the loan. This mechanism reduces the bank’s risk, as if one member 
is unable to repay the loan, the other members are obligated to assist in repayment. 
7 These loans do not require borrowers to pledge property as collateral, which 
makes them accessible to the poorest segments of the population who are 
normally shut out from traditional banking. (Para. 5) 
Meaning：These loans are designed to be accessible to the poorest individuals by 
removing the requirement for property as collateral.  
pledge:  
vt. leave sth. with sb. as a guarantee that you will repay money that you have borrowed 
抵押；典当  
He pledged his car as collateral to secure the loan from the bank. 
n. [C] (fml.) (~ of) a serious promise or agreement, esp. one made publicly or officially 
（尤指公开或正式作出的）誓言，誓约，保证 
The organization made a pledge of $1 million to support global poverty reduction 
efforts.

Translation 
全球减贫进展 
1 
进入21 世纪，尽管经济取得了显著发展，贫困依然是一个极具挑战的全球
性问题。贫困状况的多样化表现及其深层原因决定了每个国家需要根据自身
具体情况制定相应的解决方案。全球各国虽各自面临挑战，仍在采取独特的
策略，帮助本国公民摆脱贫困。 
2 
在撒哈拉以南非洲，贫困与生产力落后息息相关，对社会的许多方面造成了
系统性影响。农业发展缓慢导致作物产量低且不稳定，加剧了粮食安全问题。
同时，工业基础薄弱，制造业等经济领域难以支撑社会经济的持续增长。此
外，基础设施和公共服务也远远落后于世界其他地区。 
3 
多年来，这一地区的许多国家为应对系统性贫困做出了巨大努力。例如，莫
桑比克依据其国内的主要交通网络，设立了多个区域发展走廊，以改善基础
设施、提高农业生产力并推动经济多样化。其中，贝拉走廊在提升贝拉港现
代化水平以及优化港口与内陆地区的铁路和公路连接中发挥了重要作用。这
些改进措施减少了物流成本，简化了货物运输流程，有力地支持了莫桑比克
中部区域及其邻国将货物，尤其是农产品和矿产，高效运往国际市场。此外，
在走廊沿线投资建设的灌溉系统减少了对季节性降雨的依赖，提高了农业生
产力，进一步稳定了农民，尤其是小农户的农业生产。同时，基础设施与交
通的改善也加速了工业发展步伐，吸引了制造业和其他领域的投资。这些措
校园资料仓

25 
 
施协同作用，促进了经济多元化，增强了对经济波动的抵御能力，大幅缓解
了莫桑比克中部地区的贫困状况。 
4 
南亚地区是世界上人口最为稠密的区域之一，其贫困问题主要根植于土地资
源的分配不均。因此，该地区减贫的首要目标之一是解决无地农民的困境，
确保他们能获得改善生计和生活条件所需的资源与机会。 
5 
该地区的突出举措之一是孟加拉国的小微金融模式。这种模式直接向诸如手
工艺品制作、经贸活动、家禽牲畜养殖等非农业活动提供小额信贷，是一种
有效的脱贫手段。这些小额信贷无须借款人提供抵押，从而使得最贫困的群
体也能获得资金支持。该模式不仅助力贫困人口开展或扩大小型业务，增加
收入来源，改善生活质量，而且还帮助他们提高基础的财务管理和生活技能。
借款人通常采用小额、定期的还款方式，并在互助小组中建立起支持网络，
这既增强了他们的责任感，也增进了社区凝聚力。这一策略成效显著，帮助
数百万人摆脱了贫困的束缚，助力孟加拉国绝对贫困率从1978 年的82%大
幅降低到2018 年的11.3%。凭借这一模式，众多贫困人口成功实现了经济自
立，摆脱了贫困的恶性循环。 
6 
与撒哈拉以南非洲的系统性贫困及南亚普遍存在的农村贫困现象不同，随着
人口不断流向城市，拉丁美洲面临日益严峻的城市贫困挑战。来自农村地区
的移民往往陷入长期失业或非正式就业的困境，缺乏社会保障和稳定经济来
源，使他们极易陷入贫困陷阱。 
7 
为应对这些挑战，巴西政府采取了综合性的策略，其核心在于强调包容性发
展。该国积极促进职业教育发展，提升整体就业水平。同时，政府通过劳动
局设立了就业支持服务部门，有效利用网络和媒体资源构建人才数据库，实
时追踪劳动力市场的供需动态，搭建起顺畅的供需对接平台。 
8 
巴西的家庭补助金计划同样为这些移民家庭提供了帮助。政府依据贫困家庭
的人均月收入以及家中未成年子女的数量来确定援助金额。领取补助金的家
庭需确保学龄儿童和青少年按时入学、为0 至6 岁的儿童完成疫苗接种，并
为女性成员提供产前及产后保健服务。这一举措将有效地缓解代际贫困问
题。 
9 
总体而言，这三个地区展示了如何针对特定挑战制定量身定制的减贫计划。
尽管挑战重重，但克服困难的决心显而易见。随着这些减贫努力的持续推进，
消除贫困的目标正逐步变为现实。每一次的成功都为推动构建一个更加公平
和繁荣的全球社会带来了宝贵的希望和启示。

Roundtable discussion 
Sample answers 
1 
Brazil’s Family allowance program has been highly successful. It provides 
financial aid to poor families on the condition that they meet certain requirements, such 
as sending their children to school and getting regular health check-ups. In local 
communities, it has had a multi-faceted impact. For example, in the slums of Rio de 
Janeiro, children’s school attendance has significantly increased. Families that once 
struggled to afford necessities now achieve financial stability. This has led to a 
reduction in child labor as children are in school instead. It has also improved the overall 
health of the community since families are more likely to access healthcare services. 
The program has empowered families to break the cycle of poverty by investing in the 
education and health of the next generation. 
 
 
校园资料仓

29 
 
2 
⚫ 
China’s targeted poverty alleviation strategy, as in the case of “Cliff Village,” can 
be shared globally. By accurately identifying poor individuals and families and 
tailoring specific poverty reduction measures to their unique circumstances, China 
has achieved remarkable results. For example, in poverty-stricken mountainous 
areas, infrastructure construction such as building roads and installing power grids 
has been carried out. This experience can be useful for other countries with similar 
geographical challenges. It enables a more efficient use of resources as efforts are 
focused on those most in need. In the global context, especially in developing 
countries with poor populations in rural areas, this approach can ensure that 
poverty reduction policies reach the grassroots level effectively. 
China’s practice of developing industries with local characteristics in poor areas is 
also worth sharing. For instance, in some rural areas, the development of rural 
tourism, such as the establishment of agritainment( 农家乐) businesses, has 
provided new income sources for local villagers. In other areas, the cultivation of 
special agricultural products like high-quality tea or fruits has been promoted. This 
experience can inspire other countries to explore and develop industries based on 
their local resources, which not only increases local employment but also drives 
economic development in poverty-stricken regions. 
 
⚫ 
China has also drawn on many successful models from other countries. For 
example, Yunnan Province, taking into account the realities of its impoverished 
areas, has advanced the localized development of the Bangladeshi rural credit 
poverty alleviation model. With major loan funds provided by the Agricultural 
Development Bank and other fiscal sources, the province directly issues 
microloans to impoverished farmers through borrower groups and centers.

Communicating with the world  
Additional notes  
An effective interview about China’s poverty alleviation should meet four criteria. 
First, comprehensive preparation is key. Ss should craft a wide range of open-ended 
questions, delving deep into aspects like historical background, current policies, and 
future plans. They should also thoroughly research the topic, gathering real-life stories 
and data. Second, T should emphasize that a clear structure is essential. The interview 
should start with an engaging introduction, followed by a well-organized question-
answer session, and end with a concise summary. Third, appropriate language use 
matters. Ss should employ simple, clear, and vivid language, avoiding jargon and 
complex terms. Fourth, Ss should ensure their words can be understood globally, 
avoiding culture-specific references that might cause confusion. 
For evaluation, consider these aspects: 
⚫ 
Content: Assess the depth and relevance of the information shared, making sure 
facts and examples support the discussion. 
⚫ 
Structure: Check for a logical flow with smooth connections between different 
parts of the interview. 
⚫ 
Language: Look for effective use of language to convey ideas clearly and 
engagingly. 
⚫ 
Communication: Evaluate how well Chinese concepts are translated into 
accessible language, bridging cultural divides for global audiences. 
* Remind Ss to access the video micro-lecture in the digital textbook to learn more 
about the skill on Ucampus. 
 
Sample answers  
Interviewer: Good day! We’re really excited to be here in this amazing village that has 
achieved such remarkable poverty alleviation results. First of all, could you briefly 
introduce yourself and your role in this village’s development? 
Interviewee: Hi! I’m Li Hua. I’m currently on a summer internship here. During my 
time here, I’ve been closely observing and participating in some of the projects related 
to the village’s poverty alleviation and rural revitalization. I’ve seen firsthand the 
transformation that has taken place. 
 
校园资料仓

31 
 
Interviewer: Great. So, could you tell us about life in the village before the current 
poverty alleviation initiatives began? What were the main challenges? 
Interviewee: Well, before the poverty alleviation efforts were taken, this village was 
in a rather unfortunate situation. The land here is not very fertile, and traditional farming 
methods yielded very little. The infrastructure was poor. Roads to the market were 
poorly constructed, making the transportation of agricultural products a nightmare. 
Villagers had to carry their goods on their shoulders for miles to reach the nearest small 
market. Also, there was a lack of access to quality education and healthcare. Many 
children dropped out of school early, and the sick often had to travel long distances to 
get medical treatment. 
Interviewer: Sounds really difficult. What were the key strategies that were 
implemented to turn things around? 
Interviewee: One of the primary strategies was infrastructure improvement. The 
government invested a large amount of money to build wide, sturdy roads. This was 
crucial as it immediately improved transportation. Now, trucks can come right into the 
village to buy our agricultural products. Another important strategy was industrial 
development. We started a cooperative that focused on growing high-value cash crops 
like special herbs. The rationale behind this was to increase the income of villagers. We 
received training on how to cultivate these herbs properly, and the cooperative helped 
with marketing. 
Interviewer: Those strategies seem very effective. Can you share some specific success 
stories? 
Interviewee: Absolutely. Take the Wang family for example. Before, Mr. Wang could 
only grow a small amount of corn and rice on his small plot of land, and the income 
was barely enough to feed the family. After the road was built and the cooperative was 
established, he joined the cooperative and started growing special herbs. With the 
training and support from the cooperative, his harvest was abundant. Last year, his 
income from selling the herbs was more than five times what he used to earn from 
traditional farming. The family was able to build a new, spacious house and send their 
children to better schools in the nearby town. 
Interviewer: That’s truly inspiring. What ongoing measures are there to ensure long-
term prosperity? 
Interviewee: Currently, we’re focusing on several initiatives. In terms of industrial 
development, we’re exploring rural tourism. The village has a beautiful natural 
landscape and unique cultural traditions. We’ve built some small homestays and 
校园资料仓

32 
 
organized cultural experience activities for tourists. Training programs are also an 
important part. We’re training villagers in service-related skills for the tourism industry, 
like hospitality and cooking local delicacies. At the same time, we’re promoting e-
commerce. We’re teaching farmers how to sell their products online, which helps them 
reach a much larger market and get better prices. 
Interviewer: China’s achievements here are really impressive. Do you think China’s 
experience can be beneficial for other countries facing similar poverty issues? And 
how? 
Interviewee: China’s experience has many aspects that can be replicated. The targeted 
approach, where we identified the problems specific to this village and designed 
solutions accordingly, can be very useful. The large-scale infrastructure building, which 
is a fundamental step in connecting rural areas to the market, is also a key lesson. 
Another important aspect is the integration of different sectors like agriculture, industry, 
and tourism in the development process. By diversifying the economy, we can create 
more stable income sources for the villagers. Other countries can adapt these strategies 
to their local situations and make use of their own resources to drive development. 
Interviewer: Thank you so much for sharing all these valuable insights. We’ve learned 
about the initial poverty situation in the village, the key poverty alleviation strategies, 
the real-life success stories, the ongoing rural revitalization measures, and the global 
significance of China’s experience. 
Interviewee: You’re welcome. I hope that our village’s story can serve as a source of 
hope and inspiration for other communities around the world. With determination and 
the right strategies, poverty can be overcome, and long-term prosperity can be achieved.

## U3（33 页）

1 
 
 
U3 Pioneering progress: A dynamic world 
 
Unit overview 
Fostering continuous growth in a dynamic world is a challenge faced by all 
governments in the world. China has emerged as a pioneer in addressing the issue 
through decades of reform and opening up. This unit first explores China’s reform and 
opening up from both historical and contemporary perspectives. In Understanding 
China, a video briefly reviews China’s reform and opening-up process and 
achievements since 1978. Following this, the reading material highlights China’s 
continuous efforts to meet the new challenges in this ever-changing world. With the 
Free Trade Port initiative, Hainan stands at the forefront of China’s new round of reform 
and opening up in the new era. The unit also showcases the explorations and efforts for 
growth in other countries. The reading material in Exploring the World examines how 
African countries are making collective efforts to construct a single continental market 
under the African Continental Free Trade Area (AfCFTA). In Communicating with the 
world, Ss are guided to give an expository speech at the opening ceremony of a study 
tour to introduce China’s reform and opening up, as well as the Hainan FTP. This can 
deepen their understanding of why reform and opening up is a major reason for China’s 
dramatic growth, and why it is a key move that determines the success of Chinese 
modernization.

单元开篇 
Quote  
要进一步深化改革开放。改革开放是当代中国大踏步赶上时代的重要法宝，是
决定中国式现代化成败的关键一招。 
——2023 年12 月26 日，习近平在纪念毛泽东同志诞辰130 周年 
座谈会上的讲话 
 
Key facts about China 
The key facts in this unit highlight the great strides China has made as part of the 
government’s reform and opening-up policy, showing China’s economic 
transformation, growing global influence, and increased openness. The implications of 
the selected key facts are: 
Key fact 1: The staggering growth of China’s GDP is one of the most remarkable 
economic transformations in modern history. It highlights the effectiveness of China’s 
economic reforms and policies since 1978. The rapid growth in GDP has led to a 
quantum leap in the quality of life of the population and remarkable progress across all 
sectors of society. 
Key fact 2: The dramatic increase in China’s foreign trade demonstrates China’s central 
role in international supply chains and its strong economic ties with countries all over 
the world, which is a direct result of China’s reform and opening up. As a major driver 
of the global economy, China now has a significant influence over global trade policies, 
standards, and practices. 
Key fact 3: The extension of visa-free transit stays reflects China’s commitment to 
openness. The policy shows China’s soft power and its goodwill to other countries. The 
significant increase in inbound foreign visits allows more international visitors to learn 
about China and helps to dispel existing stereotypes. 
*T can guide Ss to access the digital textbook for the latest information and data 
on Ucampus.

popular sayings from the early days of China’s reform and opening up.

校园资料仓

4 
 
 
Understanding China 
The two materials in this section examine China’s reform and opening up, offering 
insights into both its historical development and recent advancements. The Viewing 
section outlines China’s reform and opening-up process by reviewing the three major 
steps: the 1978 reform decision (the starting point), the establishment of special export 
zones (a major strategy), and WTO entry (a milestone), providing Ss with a clear 
understanding of this historical progression. The text in this section probes into China’s 
reform and opening up in the new era. It takes the Hainan FTP as an example and 
illustrates the country’s efforts to strengthen the reform and opening up in the new era. 
Today’s young people might not find it easy to understand why setting economic 
development as a priority is a historic decision and how difficult it was for China to 
join the WTO. Lack of background knowledge might also make it difficult to 
understand expressions like “urgent need to catch up” and “class struggles.”  
When teaching the text, the focus is to help Ss “sense” China’s determination and 
efforts to deepen reform and keep the openness in the new international landscape, and 
“see” the initial achievements and potential outcomes. The text is a good example of 
effectively narrating China’s story. Its use of situation-based narratives, which combine 
precise time markers, specific locations, and detailed descriptions, creates vivid 
scenarios that engage audiences and enhance understanding. A key difficulty in 
teaching the text is guiding Ss to apply what they learn from this text – language 
knowledge, IC skills, and storytelling strategies – in their project as well as in real-
world international communication contexts.

1       
Back to the early 1980s, Shekou was undeveloped, with low-rise buildings, open 
land, and a rough industrial feel. Fast forward to the 2020s, and it’s a completely 
different world – towering skyscrapers, modern roads, green spaces, and a well-planned 
waterfront. What was once a quiet fishing and industrial area is now a buzzing urban 
hub with dense housing and commercial districts. 
This huge transformation comes down to China’s reform and opening-up policy, 
which turned Shenzhen into a Special Economic Zone and brought in businesses, 
investment, and rapid industrial growth. With strong government support, an open and 
dynamic market environment, and a wave of talent moving in for job opportunities, 
Shekou quickly became a high-tech and commercial hotspot. With smart city planning 
and cutting-edge technology, it’s no surprise the area has evolved into a global hub.

5 
 
 
Scripts 
In 1978, Deng Xiaoping visited Japan. 
For the first time, Deng experienced the momentum of Japan’s high-speed railway. 
“This feels fast. Almost felt [feels] like we are urged to run. This kind of 
transportation is just [what] we need at this moment,” Deng said. 
Deng’s thoughts about the Shinkansen revealed his belief in China’s urgent need 
to catch up. 
Between 1978 and 1979, Deng paid visits to many foreign states. After witnessing 
the standard of living in developed countries, the CPC leadership vowed to concentrate 
all efforts on economic development and lead China to build a modernized society. 
Deng once said, “We consult good practices from all developed nations. We also 
consult good practices from all Third World nations.” 
In 1978, the Third Plenary Session of the 11th Central Committee of the 
Communist Party of China was held in Beijing. This meeting set the foundation for the 
CPC to lead China in achieving the important economic transition of reform and 
opening up. During the session, the CPC leadership shifted the country’s priority from 
class struggles to economic development. 
“The Third Plenary Session of the 11th Central Committee of the CPC has gone 
down in history as a great turnaround in the history of the CPC. The most crucial 
achievement of that meeting was the decision to reform and open up.” 
One year after the Third Plenary Session, the CPC leadership approved setting up 
special export zones in Shenzhen, Zhuhai, Shantou, and Xiamen, allowing freer exports 
and trade in these selected areas. Later, they were given more policy support, such as 
tax reduction and encouragement on foreign investments, and are now known as special 
economic zones. Bold reform practices were tested in these zones, and the successful 
ones were promoted across the nation. After four decades, the most well-known 
example, Shenzhen, has become an innovative cosmopolitan city, which is home to 
many of China’s most successful tech companies. 
Another key step for opening up was the CPC’s timely decision to join the World 
Trade Organization. In 1986, China submitted a request to regain its status in the 
General Agreement on Tariffs and Trade, which later transformed into the WTO. With 
great perseverance, China managed to join the WTO after a 15-year-long negotiation. 
It injected China with a power boost toward globalization. A market with a population 
of over one billion people opened itself to the world. By 2013, China became the largest 
trading country and it contributed to over 30 percent of the world’s total economic 
growth during the period between 2012 and 2016. “Made in China” had swept the 
world. 
 
校园资料仓

6 
 
 
“The opening up of China’s economy is about more than just its own development. 
It means developing together with other countries, and sharing the achievements of 
China’s advancement with them. Good relationships with neighbors are important, but 
it’s also crucial for these neighbors to prosper as well. That’s why the opening up of 
China’s economy is not just important for China, but for the whole world.” 
The CPC’s commitment to catch up was realized through the process of reform 
and opening up, and China committed to sharing its development with the rest of the 
world. The key to this economic miracle is the CPC’s decision to keep an open mind, 
which as people say in Chinese, fang de kai. Just like President Xi Jinping said, “For a 
country, opening up is like the struggle of a chrysalis breaking free from its cocoon. 
There will be short-term pains, but such pains will create a new life.” 
 
Notes 
1 
To test the reform and opening-up policies, Deng Xiaoping proposed the idea of 
“special export zone (出口特区),” and four pilot special export zones in 
Shenzhen, Zhuhai, Shantou, and Xiamen were established in 1979. In 1980, the 
four pilot zones were renamed “special economic zone (经济特区).”  
2 
A special economic zone (SEZ) is a special area in a country where different or 
more favorable economic regulations are applied to attract foreign investment and 
generate economic growth. In 1980, China renamed the four pilot “special export 
zones” to “special economic zones.” In April 1988, the Hainan Special Economic 
Zone was established as another special economic zone in China, and in May 2010, 
Xinjiang’s Horgos and Kashi were added to the list. These SEZs adopt special 
economic policies and management systems to develop an export-oriented 
economy. They are major channels for China to boost its own economy by utilizing 
overseas capital, technology, personnel, and management expertise, and are 
windows to and models of China’s reform and opening up, driving socialist 
modernization.  
3 
The World Trade Organization (WTO) is a global organization that regulates 
and facilitates global trade. It provides a framework for negotiating trade 
agreements, resolving disputes between participating members, and ensuring trade 
flows smoothly, predictably, and freely. Headquartered in Geneva, Switzerland, 
this world’s largest international economic organization has 166 members 
representing over 98 percent of global trade and global GDP. Through negotiated 
agreements, the WTO reduces trade barriers, such as tariffs and quotas, enabling 
goods, services, and investments to flow more freely across borders. This has 
significantly facilitated globalization, encouraging economic interdependence, 
expanding markets for businesses, and integrating developing countries into the 
global economy.  
校园资料仓

7 
 
 
4 
The General Agreement on Tariffs and Trade (GATT) was a multilateral 
agreement initially signed in 1947 by 23 contracting parties to reduce trade barriers 
and promote international trade and economic recovery after World War II. In 
1995, GATT was replaced by the WTO. China participated decisively in the 
founding of GATT and officially became a contracting party to GATT on May 21, 
1948. In 1986, China applied for the resumption of its membership in GATT, and 
ultimately acceded to the WTO on December 11, 2001, after 15 years of complex 
negotiations. 
* T can guide Ss to study the multimodal annotations in the digital textbook on 
Ucampus.  
 
 
Language points 
1 For the first time, Deng experienced the momentum of Japan’s high-speed 
railway. (Para. 2) 
momentum: n. [U]  
1) the force that makes a moving object keep moving （使运动物体继续运动的）
冲力，推力，动力 
The rocket’s engine provided enough momentum to propel it into space. 
2) the ability to keep increasing, developing, or being more successful 动力；势头 
The athlete’s early victories in the tournament gave her the momentum to continue 
performing at her best. 
Note: In this sentence, “momentum” refers to the impressive force, speed, and progress 
of Japan’s high-speed railway. It conveys not just the physical motion of the train but 
also the broader sense of technological and economic advancement that the railway 
represented.  
2 With great perseverance, China managed to join the WTO after a 15-year-long 
negotiation. (Para. 9) 
perseverance: n. [U] determination to keep trying to achieve sth. in spite of difficulties 
不屈不挠；坚持不懈 
Despite facing numerous setbacks, she achieved her goal through perseverance and 
hard work. 
3 
It injected China with a power boost toward globalization. (Para. 9) 
inject: vt.  
1) add sth. new to a situation 注入；增添  
The coach tried to inject some enthusiasm into the team before the big game. 
2) put liquid, esp. a drug, into one’s body by using a special needle 注射（尤指药
校园资料仓

8 
 
 
物） 
The doctor had to inject the patient with painkillers to ease the discomfort after 
surgery. 
Note: In this context, “power boost” refers to a significant source of energy, 
momentum, or support that propels China forward in its journey toward globalization. 
It implies a strong enhancement or acceleration of China’s ability to engage with and 
integrate into the global economy and international community. 
4 
Just like President Xi Jinping said, “For a country opening up is like the 
struggle of a chrysalis breaking free from its cocoon. …” (Para. 11) 
Note: The phrase “the struggle of a chrysalis breaking free from its cocoon” is a 
metaphor comparing a difficult process of reform and opening up to the struggle of a 
chrysalis (a pupa) emerging from its cocoon. It suggests that the process is challenging 
and requires effort, but it is also necessary for growth and transformation – just as a 
butterfly must struggle to break free in order to develop strong wings and survive. The 
underlying idea is that hardship and struggle are often essential parts of progress and 
success. 
 
Viewing and understanding
