17 
 
2 
1 
The Hongqiao legislative contact station exemplifies China’s achievements in law 
popularization and public participation in several ways:  
⚫ Accessibility and openness: The station is located right on the street, inviting 
ordinary citizens to participate in discussions about laws that affect their lives. This 
accessibility contrasts with the perception of legislative processes being distant and 
bureaucratic. 
⚫ Citizen engagement: The vibrant debates held at the station, such as discussions 
on who should care for orphaned children, show that citizens are actively engaged 
in shaping legislation. This reflects a grassroots approach where the voices of the 
community are not just acknowledged but are integral to the lawmaking process. 
⚫ Representation of public opinion: The contact station acts as a conduit for public 
concerns to be communicated to lawmakers. It collects opinions through seminars 
and interviews, ensuring that citizens’ needs and issues are reflected in national 
legislation.  
Overall, the Hongqiao legislative contact station illustrates a dynamic and 
participatory legal framework that empowers citizens, demonstrating China’s 
commitment to incorporating public input into governance. 
2    
China’s Civil Code differs from earlier civil codes, like the French and German 
codes, by its responsiveness to the modern digital age and its comprehensive coverage 
of contemporary issues. While the French Civil Code represents the agricultural era and 
the German Civil Code reflects the industrial age, China’s Civil Code is tailored to the 
21st-century Internet and high-tech era. It addresses challenges unique to this era, such 
as personal data protection, which is a critical concern in today’s information-driven 
society. 
Additionally, the Civil Code includes specific provisions for environmental 
protection and ecological conservation, emphasizing the need for sustainable 
development – a focus not explicitly detailed in earlier codes. This forward-looking 
approach reflects a legal framework that is not only about regulating civil life but also 
about promoting a harmonious relationship between humans and nature. 
3 
The Civil Code represents a landmark achievement in China’s legislative progress, 
reflecting the country’s ability to adapt its legal framework to contemporary societal 
needs. Here are key points illustrating its significance: 
⚫ Comprehensive coverage: The Civil Code compiles existing laws related to civil 
life, making it a comprehensive resource for citizens. It addresses everyday issues 
such as misconduct on public transport and liability for high-altitude object 
throwing injuries, providing clear legal guidance that affects daily life.

18 
 
⚫ Modern relevance: Tailored to the challenges of the 21st century, the Civil Code 
includes provisions for personal data protection, highlighting the importance of 
privacy and security in an increasingly online world – a feature lacking in many 
other civil codes. 
⚫ Upholding rights and responsibilities: By instituting rules for the automatic 
renewal of residential land use rights and placing responsibilities on building 
managers for public safety, the Civil Code balances individual rights with social 
responsibilities. This approach fosters a shared sense of accountability among both 
citizens and public officials.  
⚫ Environmental considerations: The Civil Code also addresses ecological issues, 
emphasizing the need for environmental protection and conservation. This 
demonstrates an awareness of the interconnectedness of human and environmental 
health, promoting sustainable development. 
 
Intercultural communication skill  
Teaching suggestions 
The following teaching steps provide a structured way for developing cross-
cultural comparison skills, moving from conceptual understanding to applied practice. 
It’s especially helpful for Ss who may have limited exposure to comparative 
perspectives. Where appropriate, alternative approaches – such as using case studies, 
visual aids, or interactive discussions – are suggested to accommodate different 
learning styles and classroom dynamics. 
⚫ 
Start by explaining the concept of cross-cultural comparison to the class. Clarify 
that this method helps Ss understand and evaluate similarities and differences 
between cultures, systems, or practices. Emphasize that it’s particularly useful in 
discussions about law, politics, and societal values because it promotes mutual 
understanding, respect, and appreciation of diversity.  
⚫ 
Tell Ss that to make a successful cross-cultural comparison, they must identify 
specific elements to compare. For example, they might compare the concept of 
individual rights or property laws in China, France, and Germany. 
⚫ 
Remind Ss that when making a comparison between, for example, legal systems, 
it’s essential to consider the historical and societal context behind each system.  
⚫ 
Have Ss work in pairs to share and discuss their comparisons.  
* Remind Ss to access the video micro-lecture in the digital textbook to learn more 
about the skill on Ucampus. 
 
Sample answers 
China’s smart court system exemplifies its commitment to judicial accessibility. 
A landmark achievement is the Hangzhou Internet Court, which pioneered fully digital

19 
 
trials – from case filing to enforcement – using blockchain for evidence preservation. 
This mirrors global efforts like the European e-Justice Portal’s push for digitalization, 
but China’s model uniquely emphasizes domestic efficiency through end-to-end 
automation, whereas the EU focuses on multilingual interoperability for cross-border 
cases. This reflects the different context of the two systems: the vast population base in 
China versus the regional integration needs in Europe.

In the new era, legislative work embodies the values of fairness and justice. It 
transforms the fundamental issues that reflect the interests of the broadest masses of the 
people and their aspirations for a better life into concrete objectives of the rule of law 
through legal language. Law enforcement, as the link in the entire rule of law chain 
most closely connected to people’s daily lives, provides the public with the most direct 
and tangible experiences and perceptions of fairness and justice. Law enforcers must 
always uphold the belief in serving the people. Fairness and justice must be both 
“visible” and “perceptible,” ensuring that the public can feel fairness and justice in 
every judicial case they experience.

Critical thinking  
Sample answers  
⚫ In addition to the Miao people’s use of stones to symbolize authority, another 
ancient Chinese tradition that reflects legal thinking is the concept of li (礼), which 
refers to propriety, manners, and ethics. In traditional Chinese society, li was 
essential in maintaining social order and regulating behavior. It functioned as a 
moral code governing relationships, including those between rulers and subjects, 
and emphasized harmony and respect. This approach to regulating social 
interactions through a set of moral and ethical guidelines can be seen as a precursor 
to formal legal principles in China. 
⚫ In modern times, China has made significant strides in legal education and 
legislation. For example, the establishment of the unified National Legal 
Profession Qualification Exam system, which certifies legal professionals in 
China, has helped ensure that the judiciary is staffed by qualified and well-trained 
individuals. Another significant example is the introduction of the Civil Code in 
2021, which codifies personal rights, property rights, and family law in a 
comprehensive manner, representing a major advancement in the legal framework.  
⚫ Ancient legal traditions and ideas in China continue to shape modern society in 
important ways. These influences come from both moral philosophy and legal 
institutions. On the moral side, Confucian ideas like filial piety (孝) and li 
emphasize social harmony, duty to family, and respect for the elderly. These values 
are still seen in today’s laws about family responsibilities, care for the elderly, and 
social behavior. In China, moral expectations often work alongside legal rules to 
guide people’s actions. At the same time, legalist traditions also play a key role. 
During the Warring States Period, Shang Yang’s reforms introduced the idea that 
laws should apply equally to everyone, regardless of status. His famous act of 
“Establishing one’s credibility by rewarding people for moving a log (徙木立信)” 
showed how law was used to build government authority. Later, the Qin and Han 
dynasties developed written laws, standard legal procedures, and centralized 
enforcement – traditions that continue in modern China’s focus on legal 
codification and state governance. Together, these time-honored traditions – both 
moral and legal – help shape a modern Chinese legal system that blends ethical 
values with systematic laws to serve a rapidly modernized society.

21 
 
Exploring the world  
This section presents a personal account of jury duty in the UK, highlighting the 
juror’s role in the judicial process. The narrative emphasizes the seriousness with which 
citizens undertake their responsibilities, shedding light on the complexities of the UK’s 
jury system. While the UK emphasizes citizen participation in the judicial process 
through jury duty, China highlights grassroots involvement in legislative practices as 
demonstrated in the Hongqiao legislative contact station discussed in Understanding 
China. This link between the two sections allows for a comparative analysis of two 
legal systems that place a strong emphasis on public participation, albeit in different 
forms. 
The teaching focus of this section is to help Ss understand how both systems–jury 
duty in the UK and legislative participation in China–engage citizens in their respective 
legal processes. The primary challenge is guiding Ss to see how these systems, despite 
cultural and structural differences, share common values such as fairness, 
accountability, and the involvement of ordinary people in shaping justice. This provides 
an opportunity for cross-cultural exchange, where Ss can reflect on how diverse legal 
systems embody the universal ideals of justice, equity, and civic responsibility.  
 
 
Text: Behind the scenes of justice – my experience as a juror 
Background information 
This text provides a first-person narrative about an individual’s experience serving 
as a juror in a court trial. The narrator begins by describing receiving a summons for 
jury duty and concludes with reflections on the personal transformation that occurs 
through the process of jury service. The text explores several key aspects of the jury 
system in the UK, such as the random selection process, the potential challenges of jury 
duty, and the deep moral and ethical considerations that jurors face when reaching a 
verdict. The modern jury system took shape in 12th-century England and later spread 
through the British Empire. Today, the jury system remains most actively used in the 
United States.   
The text offers a unique perspective, taking the reader from the initial reluctance 
of being selected for jury duty to a newfound respect for the justice system. Key 
moments of the narrative involve the juror’s shift in perspective from external judgment 
based on media influence to a more evidence-based approach during deliberations.  
Jury duty is a civic responsibility in many countries. Understanding the role of 
jurors is important for Ss, as it highlights judicial fairness and peer involvement in legal 
decision-making. The text offers an opportunity to discuss civic duty and social 
responsibility. Jurors are required to set aside personal biases and follow the law as 
presented in court. This can be linked to broader discussions on ethics and the rule of 
law. The text also delves into the emotional challenges jurors face when making life-

22 
 
altering decisions, opening discussions on moral dilemmas and the justice system’s 
demand for objective decisions despite emotional or societal pressures. 
 
Notes 
1 
Jury instructions are a set of guidelines that a judge gives to a jury about the law 
that applies to a case. The purpose of jury instructions is to help the jury reach a 
verdict that follows the law.  
2 
The jury room is a private room adjacent to a courtroom, where a trial jury 
discusses a case and reaches its verdict. 
3 
A court reporter is a trained professional who writes down every word spoken 
during a court case. They create complete, word-for-word records of what judges, 
lawyers, and witnesses say. 
* T can guide Ss to study the multimodal annotations in the digital textbook on 
Ucampus.  
 
Language points 
1 
Yet, even if you are against going, should you one day receive the same orderly 
missive I had in my hands, it’s unlikely you’ll be able to dodge it. (Para. 2) 
Meaning: Even if you don’t want to go, if you receive the same official notice I did, 
it’s unlikely you’ll be able to avoid it. 
should: v. (modal verb) used after “if” or instead of “if” for describing a situation that 
may possibly happen 如果；假如 
Should you decide to attend, we would be happy to welcome you.   
Note: The structure “should you …” is an inverted construction often used in formal or 
hypothetical contexts. It means “if you” but is more conditional and formal. This 
construction is often used in written language, legal contexts, or formal communication 
to present hypothetical scenarios in a polite or elevated tone. 
dodge:  
vt. deliberately avoid discussing sth. or doing sth. （故意）避开，避免（谈论某事
或做某事） 
He managed to dodge the question by changing the subject. 
v. move quickly to avoid sb. or sth. （快速）躲开，避开 
She dodged the ball just in time. 
Note: “Dodge” can be used both in a physical sense (dodging an object) and in a more 
metaphorical sense (avoiding a responsibility or question). It is also used in everyday 
conversation with a slightly informal tone, though its usage in formal contexts can 
imply a calculated or intentional avoidance.

23 
 
2 
If you dread the prospect, take solace in the fact that the Ministry of Justice 
estimates that there is only a 35 or so percent chance of any one person being 
selected in their entire lifetime, and barely more than half of those will 
actually end up on a jury. (Para. 2) 
Meaning: If you’re worried about being called for jury duty, take comfort in knowing 
that the Ministry of Justice estimates the likelihood of any individual being selected for 
jury duty in their lifetime is only around 35 percent, and just over half of those selected 
will actually serve on a jury. 
3 
As luck would have it, I was the eighth to be dubbed a “winner” of the jury 
prize draw. (Para. 4) 
as luck would have it: used to say that sth. happened by chance 碰巧；真幸运 
As luck would have it, the rain stopped right when we reached the park. 
Note: This phrase is often used in informal or conversational language to describe 
events or situations that seem governed by fate or chance, especially when something 
ironic happens unexpectedly.  
dub: vt. (usu. passive) give sth. or sb. a name that describes them in some way 把…称
为；给…起绰号 
He was dubbed the “King of Pop” because of his musical influence. 
4 
It’s one thing to read about criminals being sent down and perhaps think 
you’d have no problem condemning them to the rest of their lives behind bars; 
they may apparently deserve it and their guilt may seem certain. But when 
faced with the harsh reality of it there right in front of you, it’s an entirely 
different situation. (Para. 6) 
It’s one thing to ..., (it’s) another thing to ...: used to say that doing one thing is very 
different from doing another thing, esp. where the second thing is more difficult, 
important, or serious …是一回事，…是另一回事 
It’s one thing to read about healthy diets, but another to follow them. 
Note: This structure is often used to show that a situation is easier in theory than in 
practice. It draws attention to the gap between reading or thinking about something and 
actually doing or experiencing it. It’s a common construction in conversational English, 
particularly in contexts where someone might feel confident in their opinions but might 
act differently when faced with a real situation.  
send down: (BrE) (infml.) send sb. to prison 送某人进监狱 
He was sent down for five years for his involvement in the fraud. 
Note: This phrase is used informally, typically in casual or journalistic contexts. It is 
mostly used in British English and may not be as common in American English, where 
the term “sent to prison” or “sentenced to jail” is preferred. It’s useful in discussions 
about legal consequences and punishments.

24 
 
behind bars: (infml.) in prison 坐牢 
The documentary sheds light on the lives of those who have spent decades behind bars. 
Note: The phrase “behind bars” is a common metaphor for prison. It’s a visual image, 
evoking the idea of being physically confined by prison bars.  
5 
Doubt creeps in. (Para. 6) 
creep in: gradually start to be noticeable 逐渐显现；逐渐引起注意 
As she thought more about the decision, hesitation crept in, making her unsure of what 
to do next. 
Note: This phrase is often used in situations where uncertainty or fear arises gradually. 
It’s a useful expression when describing emotions or psychological states that develop 
over time and are not immediately obvious. It helps convey the idea that doubt is 
something that can subtly undermine confidence or decision-making. 
6 
You listen to every word; you realize someone’s future is on the line, but 
equally difficult is that acquitting them is effectively telling their accusers, 
“We don’t believe you.” (Para. 6) 
on the line: at risk 处于危险之中；受到威胁；岌岌可危  
His job is on the line if he doesn’t meet his targets this quarter. 
acquit: vt. (usu. passive) give a decision in a court of law that sb. is not guilty of a 
crime 宣判…无罪 
Despite the overwhelming evidence, he was acquitted on the grounds of insufficient 
evidence. 
Note: Notice how the sentence contrasts two difficult aspects of decision-making using 
“but” and “equally difficult.” It effectively demonstrates the tension between these 
challenges and shows how to structure sentences that present contrasting ideas. In legal 
contexts, “acquitting” is a formal term meaning clearing someone of charges. It carries 
significant moral and legal implications, as highlighted in this sentence.  
7 
Through every emotional witness called to the stand, each 45-minute speech 
delivered by the prosecution and defence barristers, not to mention the line-
by-line analysis of police interviews, any doubts I had about the legitimacy of 
the jury system in the modern age dissipated like morning fog. (Para. 7) 
Note: The sentence is complex and cumulative, combining multiple descriptive phrases 
that build on one another to convey a gradual shift in the narrator’s perspective. It begins 
with a series of actions / events (“witnesses,” “speeches,” “analyses”), which 
collectively lead to a result (“the dissipation of doubts”). The sequence of events builds 
toward the conclusion, where the narrator reflects on the change in her perception of 
the jury system. The use of prepositional phrases (“through,” “by”) helps to structure 
the progression of events, creating a sense of anticipation or suspense before revealing 
the outcome.

25 
 
Translation  
司法的背后——我的陪审员经历 
1 
一天下午，当我正在翻看一叠看似常规的邮件时，发现了一个看起来相当
正式的信封，上面写着“英国皇家法院及法庭服务处”的字样。信封里是
一张传票，要求我履行陪审员义务，约七周后前往地方法院参与可能持续
十天甚至更久的陪审工作。 
2 
我屡次听到人们抱怨被传唤担任陪审员——既耽误工夫，又影响工作收入，
诸如此类。然而，即便你不愿意去，如果某一天你也收到了像我手中那样
的正式公函，你也很难逃避。凡年满18 周岁的公民都随时可能会被传召—
—这是从选民名册中随机抽取的——如今只有少数几种情况可构成合理的
豁免理由，例如在刑事司法系统工作。若y 遇到与你长期计划的假期冲突
等情形，你可以申请延期履行；但其他唯一的逃避方式就是你是被定罪的
罪犯。如果你对这一前景感到畏惧，可以安慰自己，据司法部估计，一个
人一生中被选中担任陪审员的概率只有大约35%，而其中只有过半的人最终
会成为真正的陪审员。 
3 
那一天终于到来了。我沿着市政厅街漫步，随意走进了那个未知的世界。
法院工作人员态度友好、语气轻松地迎接我们这些准陪审员，并检查是否
所有人都到齐。通常会有几个备用陪审员，以防最终的12 名陪审员出现任
何问题。 
4 
我们坐在长椅上，随后被指示，如果我们的名字从抽签箱中被抽中，就到
陪审席就座。法官告诉我们，如果没有被选中也不要感到沮丧。运气不错，
我是第八个被抽中的“幸运儿”。 
5 
在听取了陪审团指示后，我们便立即投入庭审。这是一起无论从哪个角度
看都特别恶劣的案件，以我日常接触社交媒体暴民的经验，绝对是那种会
让很多人从第一分钟开始就愤怒不已、疯狂敲击键盘打出“绞死他们”的
案件。 
6 
我曾也会不假思索地认为被告有罪，但一旦成为司法系统的一部分，我的
整个观点都发生了转变。在新闻报道中读到罪犯被判刑时，你可能会觉得
判处他们终身监禁没什么困难——他们似乎是罪有应得，而罪行似乎也确
凿无疑。然而，当这种残酷的现实活生生展现在你面前时，情况就完全不
同了。怀疑悄悄滋生。你仔细听每一个字，意识到某人的未来就掌握在你
的手中，但同样艰难的是，如果判其无罪，就相当于告诉控方：“我们不相
信你”。 
7 
每一位陪审员都显然以同样严肃的态度对待这项任务。通过每一位情绪激
动的证人证言，控辩双方律师每一场45 分钟的陈述，更不用说对警方讯问

26 
 
记录的逐字逐句分析，我对现代陪审制度合理性的所有疑虑都如晨雾般消
散。 
8 
当双方都已陈述完毕，法官详细总结证据后，我们退回陪审团评议室开始
讨论案件。我们认真对待这一任务，仔细审查证据，反复查阅笔记，还请
法庭书记员进来，让我们再次听取某些证词。整个过程严格限制在陪审团
评议室内，不受任何外界干扰。我们的讨论内容永远不得外泄，我们也无
须以任何方式为我们的裁决提供理由。经过一整天的审议，我们最终达成
了裁决。 
9 
我们最后一次列队走进法庭时，被告、辩护律师和控方都站了起来。宣读
完裁决结果，法官感谢我们为司法服务。就这样，为期十天的陪审员工作
画上句号。 
10 回望这段经历，我由衷庆幸自己被选中履行作为公民的责任。陪审工作既
富有教育意义，又令人受益匪浅。当一切结束时，我感觉自己确实有所作
为，成为了某种更崇高事业的一部分。这段陪审员经历让我重新认识到司
法系统的公正严明，以及司法工作者的责任担当与专业精神。 
 
Reading and understanding  
Reference answers 
1  
1) 18  
2) randomly 
3) a summons 
4) dodge  
5) 12  
2  
The correct order is: b, c, e, a, d 
3  
1 
On the way to the court, the author feels casual and somewhat indifferent, as 
reflected by the sentence, “I strolled down Town Hall Street and casually walked 
into the unknown.” However, after being chosen as one of the 12 jurors, the author 
seems to feel a mix of surprise and perhaps subdued satisfaction, as she describes 
being the “eighth to be dubbed a ‘winner’ of the jury prize draw.” There is no 
strong sense of dread or excitement but rather a sense of inevitability and 
responsibility.

27 
 
2 
From a non-juror perspective, the author initially feels strongly influenced by the 
social media mob mentality, where people quickly assume guilt and demand harsh 
punishment without fully understanding the facts. The author admits that, before 
becoming part of the judicial process, she would have instantly assumed guilt and 
reacted emotionally, similar to the online crowd. This perspective reflects a lack of 
critical evaluation and reliance on public sentiment rather than evidence. 
3 
The author takes her role as a juror very seriously. She recognizes the weight of 
the responsibility and reflects on the emotional impact of the case, noting how her 
perspective shifted from assuming guilt to experiencing doubt as the trial 
progressed. The author feels the burden of being part of the judicial process, 
understanding that the jury’s verdict can affect someone’s future. Throughout, she 
describes the process with a sense of duty and professionalism, emphasizing that 
every juror approached the task with seriousness and care. 
4 
The author reflects positively on the importance of the jury system, stating that it 
helped her appreciate its fairness and rigor. She mentions that her experience as a 
juror gave her a deeper understanding of the responsibility involved, as well as the 
professionalism of those who work within the system. The author concludes with 
a sense of fulfillment, feeling that her participation made a difference and that she 
was part of something bigger, which ultimately affirmed her belief in the integrity 
of the system.

Live interview 
Teaching suggestions 
This task helps Ss practice structured speaking on legal topics while developing 
intercultural communication skills. It focuses on three key areas: access to legal 
services, the law’s impact on daily life, and the role of citizens in promoting social 
justice. To support Ss, T can provide background on legal systems in other countries 
and encourage the use of transition phrases (e.g., first, in addition, on the one hand) for 
structured responses. Challenges may include expressing abstract concepts like justice 
or making meaningful comparisons. To address this, Ss can use simple, real-life 
examples. Overall, the activity fosters critical thinking and cultural awareness. As an 
extension, Ss can write a short comparison report or conduct a follow-up Q&A session 
based on their role-play. 
 
Sample answers 
Host: Good evening, and welcome to Law and Life! I’m joined by two distinguished 
guests: Mr. Li from China and Mr. Johnson from the UK. Tonight, we’ll be discussing 
how legal systems and access to justice differ between their countries. Let’s dive right 
in. Mr. Li, how do people in China access legal information and services? 
Mr. Li: In China, there are several ways people can access legal information and 
services. For example, we have government websites and mobile apps where people 
can find laws and regulations easily. Additionally, the legal aid system supports those 
who cannot afford legal representation. Local legal advice stations have also been set 
up in many communities, offering free consultations. And, of course, there’s a strong

30 
 
presence of law firms that provide paid legal services for more specialized needs.  
Host: That’s very informative. Mr. Johnson, how does the situation compare in the 
UK? How do people access legal information and services there? 
Mr. Johnson: In the UK, legal information is mainly available through online 
resources, such as government websites, where people can find comprehensive guides 
on a wide range of legal issues. We also have a robust system of Citizens Advice 
Bureaus, where people can go for free and impartial advice. Legal services are generally 
provided by solicitors. The legal aid system exists for individuals who meet specific 
eligibility criteria, making sure legal help isn’t limited to the wealthy. 
Host: It seems like both countries have accessible systems, though in different ways. 
Now, let’s move on to the next question. Mr. Li, how does the law affect daily life and 
decision-making for individuals in China? 
Mr. Li: In China, the law plays a significant role in shaping people’s daily lives. For 
instance, laws around property, contracts, and labor rights influence personal decisions 
like buying a home, starting a business, or choosing a career path. The recently 
introduced Civil Code further emphasizes the protection of individual rights, including 
privacy and data security. The law also governs public services in health, education, 
and social welfare, making it a guiding factor in personal decision-making. 
Host: That’s interesting! Mr. Johnson, how does the law impact people’s daily lives in 
the UK? 
Mr. Johnson: In the UK, the law influences many aspects of daily life, ranging from 
family matters like marriage and divorce to employment or consumer rights. For 
example, employment laws help ensure fair pay and protection against discrimination, 
influencing workplace decisions. Consumer protection laws give people confidence 
when they buy goods and services. The law in the UK also safeguards personal 
freedoms and rights in public spaces.  
Host: It’s fascinating to see how legal frameworks shape lives in different ways. 
Finally, let’s talk about the role of ordinary citizens. Mr. Li, what role do ordinary 
citizens in China play in promoting and maintaining social justice? 
Mr. Li: Chinese citizens participate actively in promoting social justice, especially 
through grassroots organizations and community legal stations. People are encouraged 
to report legal violations and seek justice for themselves and others. Additionally, China 
has implemented various public legal education programs to raise awareness of 
citizens’ rights and responsibilities. Moreover, citizens are increasingly involved in 
providing feedback on legal reforms and policies, contributing to judicial review and 
fairness. 
Host: That’s a powerful role! Mr. Johnson, what about in the UK? How do ordinary 
citizens contribute to social justice? 
Mr. Johnson: In the UK, citizens also play an essential role in promoting social justice. 
For instance, they can participate in public consultations regarding new laws or

31 
 
amendments to existing ones. Public campaigns, activism, and the work of charities and 
NGOs are crucial in raising awareness about social justice issues. Furthermore, the right 
to vote and involvement in local government enable citizens to shape policies that 
protect vulnerable groups in society. Citizens can also act as jurors in the judicial 
system, ensuring that justice is served fairly. 
Host: It’s clear that in both countries, the role of ordinary citizens is crucial for 
promoting fairness and social justice. Thank you, Mr. Li and Mr. Johnson, for sharing 
your valuable insights. It’s been a pleasure discussing these important topics with both 
of you!

32 
 
Communicating with the world  
Additional notes  
To create a compelling publicity video on China’s rule of law, Ss should focus on 
delivering a clear, engaging message. Start by highlighting key topics such as legal 
reforms, public participation in lawmaking, and technological advances like online 
courts. Present these themes in a way that connects with universal principles of justice 
and fairness, making them relevant to an international audience. The video should open 
with a strong hook, like an intriguing question or fact, to capture attention. The script 
should focus on two or three key points, such as legislative contact stations and the 
Civil Code, using concrete examples to show their impact on daily life. Storytelling is 
essential – relatable anecdotes can emotionally connect viewers to the message. Visuals 
must support the message, using footage of legislative activities, everyday scenarios, or 
legal technologies. The on-screen text should emphasize key points, while clear 
narration and well-paced background music could be used to enhance the viewer’s 
experience. Conclude with a strong, memorable statement that reinforces the 
importance of China’s legal advancements in building a fairer, safer society. This 
approach will help Ss create an informative and impactful video. 
For evaluation, focus on content relevance, structure, language clarity, visual 
alignment, and cultural engagement. 
*Remind Ss to access the video micro-lecture in the digital textbook to learn more 
about the skill on Ucampus. 
 
Sample answers  
Title: China’s path to the rule of law: Building a fair and just society 
[Scene 1: Opening] 
Narration: 
In a country of over 1.4 billion people, the rule of law shapes how daily life unfolds – 
how we interact, how we live, and how we work. But how did this transformation take 
place in China, and what’s next for its legal system? 
 
[Scene 2: Citizen engagement in lawmaking] 
Narration: 
One of China’s key achievements has been bringing the law closer to the people. Just 
like the UK’s jury system, where citizens actively participate in trials, China encourages 
its citizens to take part in lawmaking through grassroots channels. At places like the 
Hongqiao legislative contact station, citizens can directly engage with lawmaking – 
offering suggestions, raising concerns, and even pushing for change. This unique 
approach fosters a sense of ownership and responsibility in the process of lawmaking.

33 
 
[Scene 3: Legal advancements for the modern age] 
Narration: 
A major step forward in China’s legal development is the Civil Code – the country’s 
first unified civil code, enacted in 2021. It brings together laws on property, personality 
rights, contracts, and more into one comprehensive system. Covering nearly every 
aspect of civil life, the Code strengthens legal protections, reflects modern social needs, 
and marks a key moment in building a fair, well-ordered society under the rule of law.  
 
[Scene 4: Technology and the judiciary] 
Narration: 
China has also embraced technology to enhance its legal system. From online courts to 
AI-assisted legal tools, innovations are making the judicial process faster, more 
efficient, and more accessible to everyone. These technological advancements are not 
only improving how the law is applied – they’re improving how citizens access and 
understand the law, breaking down the barriers to justice. 
 
[Scene 5: Impact on daily life] 
Narration: 
The impact of these advancements is felt daily. From ensuring safe public spaces to 
addressing issues like digital privacy and property rights, China’s rule of law is 
transforming the lives of its people. For example, in cities across China, smartphone 
apps now help people settle disputes, file complaints, and access legal services without 
leaving home. Whether it’s resolving a neighborhood conflict or claiming unpaid 
wages, these tools make justice faster and easier to reach. It’s through changes like 
these that legal reform becomes real, shaping a fairer, more secure society for everyone. 
 
[Scene 6 Conclusion] 
Narration: 
As China continues to develop, its legal system moves with it, creating a society built 
on fairness, security, and shared responsibility. The rule of law is no longer just a 
concept; it’s a way of life that empowers every citizen. Together, we’re building a 
future of justice, equality, and opportunity.

34

## U5（28 页）

1 
 
 
U5 For a shared future 
 
Unit overview 
The title “For a shared future” emphasizes cooperation, mutual benefit, and global 
responsibility. It reflects the vision of building an interconnected world where nations work 
together to address common challenges and achieve sustainable development. The phrase 
suggests that the future is not determined by any single country but rather by collective efforts, 
highlighting the importance of cooperation, multilateralism, and cultural exchange in fostering 
peace and prosperity. This unit explores China’s diplomatic principles and initiatives aimed at 
building a better world, as well as global efforts to achieve a prosperous, secure, and civilized 
future. The Understanding China section introduces Zheng He, China’s renowned sea explorer, 
whose seven voyages promoted peace and cultural exchange across more than 30 countries, 
influencing China’s foreign policy today. It also traces China’s diplomatic evolution from the 
1955 Bandung Conference to its commitment to a global community of shared future. The 
Exploring the World section is about the ways countries around the globe attain a thriving, 
safe, and culturally advanced future. Additionally, in Communicating with the World, Ss are 
asked to write a position paper on China’s vision for a better world. 
 
思政元素清单 
Understanding China — Viewing 
 
和平发展是中国长期以来一贯坚持的政策。 
 
推动共建“一带一路”更高质量发展，构建全球发展共同体。 
Understanding China — Reading 


单元开篇 
Quote  
中国始终是世界和平的建设者、全球发展的贡献者、国际秩序的维护者、公共产品的提
供者，将继续以中国的新发展为世界提供新机遇。 
——2021 年9 月21 日，习近平在第七十六届联合国大会一般性辩论上的讲话 
 
Key facts about China 
The key facts in this unit are closely related to major-country diplomacy with Chinese 
characteristics. Each key fact highlights one or two aspects of China’s diplomatic efforts and 
achievements.   
Key fact 1: This fact indicates that China’s Global Development Initiative (GDI), Global 
Security Initiative (GSI), and Global Civilization Initiative (GCI) are being implemented at an 
accelerated pace. It highlights the global acceptance and recognition of China’s contributions 
to addressing common global challenges. Here, the Group of Friends of the Global 
Development Initiative means “全球发展倡议之友小组” in Chinese. As an important 
platform for cooperation based on the principle of extensive consultation, joint contribution, 
and shared benefits, the Group of Friends of the Global Development Initiative represents a 
major step forward in building the mechanism for GDI cooperation.  
Key fact 2: This fact reflects China’s expanding global diplomatic relationships and its 
commitment to strengthening international cooperation. 
Key fact 3: This fact shows China’s long-standing tradition of supporting global development 
through tangible infrastructure projects, exemplified by the historical construction of the 
Tanzania-Zambia Railway in Africa.  
* T can guide Ss to access the digital textbook for the latest information and data on 
Ucampus. 
 
Teaching suggestions 
The following steps help Ss develop a nuanced understanding of China’s diplomatic 
principles and initiatives. The sequence then transitions from theoretical concepts to practical 
actions, effectively capturing Ss’ interest and equipping them for the unit project.  
Step 1: Start by presenting the unit title. Ask Ss to briefly discuss what the title means. Guide 
Ss to interpret the quote of the unit, emphasizing China’s diplomatic stance and global 
contributions in four dimensions.  
⚫ A builder of world peace: China advocates peaceful coexistence, seeks diplomatic 
resolutions to conflicts, and promotes international stability through cooperation.

3 
 
⚫ A contributor to global development: China actively participates in international 
initiatives and provides substantial aid, investment, and development assistance aimed at 
improving global prosperity and well-being. 
⚫ A defender of the international order: China supports multilateralism, respects 
international norms, and upholds the role of global institutions like the UN in maintaining 
fairness and equity. 
⚫ A provider of public goods: China actively delivers global public resources, such as 
infrastructure, health assistance, and humanitarian aid, benefiting the international 
community. 
Step 2: Have Ss review and retell the key facts about China’s diplomatic efforts and 
achievements. Organize them into small groups to discuss which aspect of China’s diplomatic 
efforts and achievements is reflected in each fact. 
Step 3: Use the Scenario to stimulate Ss’ global and intercultural thinking, enabling them to 
talk about China’s diplomatic stance and describe China’s achievements and the measures it 
has taken toward realizing this vision.  
Step 4: Have Ss read the unit’s learning objectives, and explain what these objectives mean 
and how they relate to the unit’s content. Remind Ss that these objectives serve as a guide for 
self-assessment, helping them evaluate if they can achieve them after learning this unit.

4 
 
Understanding China 
Both materials in this section touch upon the theme of China’s diplomacy, the main aims 
of which are peace and cooperation. The video exemplifies ancient China’s peaceful diplomacy, 
as well as its implications in modern times. The reading material reviews China’s diplomatic 
journey since the founding of the People’s Republic of China, highlighting the country’s 
continued commitment to peaceful development, cooperation, multilateralism, and active 
participation in initiatives such as the Belt and Road Initiative, global development, security, 
and civilization initiatives, all aimed at building a global community of shared future. From the 
video to the text, the materials combine to show how China has inherited the spirit of peaceful 
development and how it advances this spirit in its modern diplomatic practices.  
The teaching of the video material should emphasize the peaceful principles of Zheng 
He’s voyages, their significance, and China’s contemporary diplomatic practices aimed at 
promoting the world’s peaceful development. The teaching of the text should focus on key 
modern diplomatic events and China’s evolving international role. Teaching difficulties may 
lie in linking ancient and modern diplomacy and analyzing the changes in China’s role and 
diplomatic impact. 
The materials provide an opportunity to explore how China contributes to world peace 
and development, how China safeguards the international order, and what public goods China 
provides to the world. These initiatives align with the concept of building a global community 
of shared future, a key aspect of President Xi Jinping’s vision for global governance.

5 
 
⚫ Video prediction 
Based on these images, it can be inferred that the video explores China’s historical 
and modern maritime activities, emphasizing the enduring spirit of peaceful development 
passed down from the past to the present. It may start with Zheng He’s historical voyages, 
highlighting China’s early explorations and peaceful diplomatic efforts. The video might 
then transition to modern times, showing how these historical principles influence current 
naval operations and international cooperation.  
 
Scripts 
China’s greatest naval explorer, Zheng He, commanded fleets on seven voyages during 
the Ming Dynasty. Between 1405 and 1433, his huge fleet arrived in over 30 countries in 
Southeast Asia, South Asia, and even Mogadishu in East Africa. These unprecedented voyages 
were characterized by advanced shipbuilding and navigational techniques. They predated 
Christopher Columbus’ discovery of the new continent by more than half a century. It is widely 
believed that Zheng He’s expeditions heralded the Grand Voyages. Even with the largest fleet 
in the world at that time, China had no desire to exploit or plunder other lands and indeed did 
not. Instead, Zheng He’s fleet showed respect to all they encountered. In fact, they carried 
messages of friendship, helped locals build cities and combat piracy, and imparted skills such 
as shipbuilding and farming. Sailors took with them a treasure trove of exquisite goods, 
including silk and porcelain. They were also treated with local specialties. For instance, Zheng 
He was introduced to the aromatic taste of coffee, making him the first Chinese to savor this 
beverage. He was also the first person from the East to encounter Africa’s majestic animal of 
giraffe.  
Nowadays, in many places along his routes, Zheng He is still regarded as an embodiment 
of peace and friendship. When President Xi Jinping visited Kazakhstan and Indonesia in 2013, 
he proposed the major initiatives of building the Silk Road Economic Belt and the 21st-Century 
Maritime Silk Road. The name Zheng He came up many times, with Xi proudly presenting the 
voyages to the world as a business card of China.  
Indeed, an edict accompanying Zheng He on his voyages set out principles for treating all 
nations equally, counseling against the strong bullying the weak, and promoting peace. In 1998, 
the National Geographic magazine named Zheng He as one of the navigators of the 
millennium. In laying out the reason, it said: “He led the largest fleet on Earth in the 15th 
century, consisting of nearly 30,000 battle-hardened soldiers, yet did not occupy an inch of 
foreign land.” 
The world today is undergoing profound changes unseen in a century. China cleaves 
steadfastly to the spirit of Zheng He. China has always adhered to the principles of peace, 
development, cooperation, and mutual benefit, adhered to the path of peaceful development, 
and worked to build a community of shared future for mankind. Since 2008, the PLA Navy has 
often dispatched fleets to carry out escort missions in the Gulf of Aden. These missions have

6 
 
involved providing security escorts and conducting rescue operations for both Chinese and 
other ships in the region. The navy has also built a hospital ship, the famous Peace Ark, for 
maritime medical rescue operations. This vessel has ventured beyond national waters, 
delivering medical aid to many countries. Zheng He’s voyages foreshadowed what his country 
would give to the world: diplomacy based on peaceful exchanges and cultural interactions. Six 
centuries after the expeditions, we can rightly continue to marvel at the feats of Zheng He and 
his crew. We can also be thankful that their compatriots of the 21st century learned from the 
example: Keep the flag of peace flying high. 
 
Notes 
The Peace Ark is the first standard ocean-going maritime hospital independently designed and 
built by China. This floating medical center has traveled across the seas to deliver humanitarian 
aid and free medical services to countries along the coastlines. Since commencing “Mission 
Harmony” in August 2010, it has visited 49 countries and regions, providing medical services 
to over 370,000 individuals. The hospital ship stands as a powerful symbol of a maritime 
community with a shared future.  
 
Language points 
1 
They predated Christopher Columbus’ discovery of the new continent by more than 
half a century. 
predate: v. happen or exist earlier in history than sth. else（历史上）早于…发生或出现 
The tradition of storytelling predates written language, as ancient cultures relied on oral 
narratives to preserve their history. 
Note: This sentence places Zheng He’s voyages in a global historical context and compares the 
timing of his voyages to that of Christopher Columbus’, highlighting the significance of Zheng 
He’s expeditions. 
2 
Even with the largest fleet in the world at that time, China had no desire to exploit 
or plunder other lands and indeed did not. 
Note: This sentence contrasts China’s military capability with its peaceful intentions, 
highlighting China’s peaceful approach to exploration. This counters common stereotypes that 
associate rising powers with imperialistic expansion. 
3 
Indeed, an edict accompanying Zheng He on his voyages set out principles for 
treating all nations equally, counseling against the strong bullying the weak, and 
promoting peace.  
counsel against: advise one against taking some action 建议拒绝 
I was counseled against going for a walk alone at night.

7 
 
4 
Keep the flag of peace flying high. 
keep the flag of … flying: support, defend, promote, or highlight the positive characteristics 
of sb. or sth. 支持；捍卫；维护 
They had formed a literary club to keep the flag of culture flying.

Intercultural communication skill 
Teaching suggestions 
The purpose of these steps is to help Ss comprehend the concept and significance of 
cultural icons, learn how to identify and research them, and analyze their symbolic meanings. 
Furthermore, the focus is on guiding Ss to explore the enduring relevance of cultural icons 
across different periods and regions, and to connect them with contemporary issues, thereby 
enhancing their cross-cultural communication skills. 
⚫ 
Begin by defining cultural icons and discussing their significance in representing a 
culture’s values and beliefs. 
⚫ 
Show examples of well-known cultural icons from different countries and discuss their 
meanings and impacts, such as the pyramids in Egypt. 
⚫ 
Guide Ss in identifying China’s cultural icons that are recognized both nationally and 
internationally. Assign Ss to study successful examples (e.g., Zheng He) and explore the 
reasons behind their global resonance (e.g., historical impact, cross-cultural relevance).

8 
 
Compare lesser-known icons (e.g., wax printing) with globally recognized ones (e.g., the 
Great Wall) to discuss recognition barriers. 
⚫ 
Guide Ss to research and analyze the deeper meanings and historical contexts of cultural 
icons. Have Ss practice explaining the connotations of selected icons, focusing on their 
symbolic significance and the stories they represent. 
⚫ 
Help Ss explore how cultural icons remain relevant across different periods and regions. 
Encourage Ss to find connections between historical icons and contemporary issues, such 
as linking Zheng He’s voyages to modern international cooperation. 
* Remind Ss to access the video micro-lecture in the digital textbook to learn more about 
the skill on Ucampus. 
 
Sample answers 
3 
⚫ 
Bruce Lee  
Bruce Lee, an iconic figure of Chinese culture, was a martial arts legend and connected 
the East and the West through films like Enter the Dragon. His philosophy of Jeet Kune Do 
emphasizes adaptability and self-expression, breaking free from traditional constraints. In 
cinema, he portrayed invincible heroes, showcasing the depth of Chinese martial arts and 
challenging Western stereotypes of Asian people. His influence transcended borders, inspiring 
countless martial artists and filmmakers globally. Even today, his legacy endures in martial arts 
training methods, film narratives, and pop culture, symbolizing the fusion of Eastern 
philosophy with Western pop culture. His spirit of perseverance and innovation continues to 
resonate, serving as a source of pride for the Chinese people and a bridge connecting diverse 
cultures. 
⚫ 
The Great Wall  
Constructed across many dynasties beginning in the 7th century BCE, the Great Wall was 
originally a military defensive system, embodying the indomitable spirit of perseverance of the 
Chinese nation. It protected trade routes along the ancient Silk Road, fostering dialogue and 
mutual learning between Chinese and Western civilizations. Today, embodying the Great Wall 
spirit of perseverance, resilience, and unity, the Chinese people remain dedicated to advancing 
national rejuvenation and fostering global harmony.

9 
 
Reading 
Text: Weaving a tapestry for a better world 
Background information 
Under the guidance of Xi Jinping Thought on Diplomacy, China has made significant 
strides in shaping its foreign policy and achieving remarkable diplomatic successes. President 
Xi’s vision emphasizes building a global community of shared future, promoting global peace, 
fostering mutual respect, and advancing common development. These principles have become 
the cornerstone of China’s approach to international relations, enabling the country to play an 
increasingly influential role on the global stage. 
One of the most notable achievements under this framework is the Belt and Road Initiative 
(BRI), which has become a remarkable real-life endorsement of the nation’s major-country 
diplomacy with Chinese characteristics. By connecting over 150 countries through 
infrastructure development, trade, and investment, the BRI has strengthened economic ties, 
enhanced connectivity, and fostered shared prosperity across continents. This initiative 
exemplifies China’s commitment to win-win cooperation and inclusive development, offering 
opportunities for countries worldwide to participate in the global economy. 
China has also demonstrated leadership in addressing pressing global challenges. It has 
taken decisive action in combating climate change, such as pledging to achieve carbon 
neutrality by 2060 and actively participating in international climate agreements. In the realm 
of public health, China has provided critical support to countries by supplying medical 
equipment, vaccines, and expertise, demonstrating its dedication to global solidarity. 
Additionally, China’s efforts in poverty alleviation and sustainable development have set an 
example for other nations, contributing to the United Nations’ Sustainable Development Goals. 
Through its active engagement in multilateral diplomacy, including its participation in the 
United Nations, G20, BRICS, and other international organizations, China has advocated for a 
fairer and more inclusive global governance system. By promoting dialogue and cooperation 
in resolving regional conflicts and fostering stability, China has strengthened its position as a 
responsible major power. 
Xi Jinping Thought on Diplomacy has guided China in forging partnerships based on 
equality, mutual benefit, and respect for sovereignty, building a new type of international 
relations. China’s diplomatic achievements under this framework highlight its commitment to 
building a more equitable, peaceful, and interconnected world. 
 
Notes  
The “BRICS+” cooperation model is an initiative introduced by China to expand the scope 
of collaboration beyond the original BRICS member countries (Brazil, Russia, India, China, 
and South Africa). It aims to include other emerging economies and developing countries in 
various forms of cooperation. The model seeks to build a broader network of partnerships by

10 
 
engaging non-BRICS countries, particularly those from the Global South, in dialogues and 
projects. It aims to strengthen ties between BRICS nations and other developing countries and 
to create a more inclusive and representative platform for addressing global challenges, such 
as economic inequality, sustainable development, and global governance reform. The 
“BRICS+” model has enabled BRICS to extend its influence by fostering stronger ties with 
other nations, enhancing its role as a platform for developing countries to voice their concerns 
and interests on the global stage. 
* T can guide Ss to study the multimodal annotations in the digital textbook on Ucampus.  
 
Language points  
1 
In the golden light of a tropical morning, Bandung was brimming with anticipation. 
(Para. 1) 
Meaning: In the bright tropical morning, Bandung was full of hope and excitement. 
Note: The phrase “golden light” refers to the warm, soft, and glowing sunlight that is 
characteristic of a tropical morning in Bandung. It evokes a sense of beauty, warmth, hope, 
optimism, tranquility, and a fresh start, as mornings with golden sunlight are often associated 
with peaceful and uplifting moments. It also sets a positive tone for the anticipation and 
excitement mentioned later in the sentence. 
brim with: be full of sth. 充溢；充满 
Her eyes brimmed with tears of joy as she received the unexpected gift. 
2 
The world was split by Cold War tensions, yet within the grand walls of the 
Independence Building, leaders from 29 Asian and African nations and regions 
gathered under the banner of unity. (Para. 1) 
Meaning: At a time when the world was divided by the Cold War, leaders from 29 Asian and 
African nations and regions gathered in the Independence Building, driven by a shared vision 
of unity. 
under the banner of: used when stating the principle or belief that makes sb. do sth. 在…旗
帜下；在…口号下 
The organization works under the banner of environmental protection. 
Note: The Independence Building was the venue of the Bandung Conference. T can guide Ss 
to examine the illustration in the Student’s Book and encourage them to talk more about the 
conference. 
3 
Beneath the surface of hope, however, the air was tense. (Para. 1) 
Note: This sentence contains imagery, particularly abstract imagery. It uses descriptive 
language to evoke feelings and create a vivid emotional atmosphere in readers’ mind. 
Imagery is a literary device used in poetry, novels, and other writing that uses description that

11 
 
appeals to the readers’ senses to create an image or idea in their head. The phrase “beneath the 
surface of hope” creates a vivid mental picture, suggesting that hope is like a visible layer, 
while tension lies hidden underneath. This imagery helps the readers see the emotional contrast. 
Imagery can be created using rhetorical devices like similes, metaphors, or onomatopoeia.  
This sentence employs the following rhetorical devices to create imagery: 1) Contrast 
(Antithesis): The sentence contrasts “hope” with “tense air,” showing the difference between 
outward optimism and hidden tension. This juxtaposition emphasizes the complexity of the 
situation, where positive appearances mask hidden tensions. 2) Metaphor: “surface of hope” 
metaphorically represents the outward appearance of hope. These rhetorical devices work 
together to create a nuanced description, conveying both the visible optimism and hidden 
tension in the situation. A similar example is as follows: 
Her smile was warm and bright, yet it masked the quiet storm of doubt within. 
4 
Fierce debates simmered and dissent was brewing, particularly as voices of 
opposition against communist states began to rise. (Para. 1) 
Note: In this sentence, “simmered” describes the debates as a state that was already established 
and ongoing, which aligns with the past tense, while “was brewing” describes dissent as an 
action in progress, which aligns with the past progressive tense. The use of “simmered” in the 
past tense and “was brewing” in the past progressive tense reflects the distinction between a 
static state (debates that were already happening) and a dynamic process (dissent that was still 
forming). This combination effectively conveys the nuanced relationship between the two 
actions in the sentence. 
5 
On the grand stage of the UN, the curtain rose on a new era of diplomacy for China, 
revealing a dynamic scene of international cooperation. (Para. 3) 
Note: The phrase “the curtain rose” is a theatrical metaphor, likening the start of China’s new 
diplomatic era to the opening of a stage performance. At the theater, the curtain rises at the 
beginning of the first act, marking the start of what is about to unfold. Similarly, “on” here 
connects the metaphorical action (the curtain rising) to the subject being revealed (a new era 
of diplomacy). “The curtain rose on” is a standard idiomatic phrase in English, often used 
metaphorically to describe the beginning or unveiling of something significant. It’s a figurative 
way of saying that something new or important has started. 
6 
Currently, about 30 countries are knocking on the “BRICS door.” (Para. 4) 
Note: The metaphor “knocking on the ‘BRICS door’” symbolizes that these countries are 
expressing interest or making efforts to join the BRICS group, much like someone knocking 
on a door to request entry.  
7 
This spirit of cooperation was evident in the golden-hued autumn of 2013 when 
President Xi Jinping visited Kazakhstan and Indonesia and unveiled a new vision 
that harked back to the legendary Silk Road. (Para. 5)

12 
 
Note: Literally, “golden-hued” refers to the rich golden tones found in autumn. Symbolically, 
the “golden-hued autumn” represents a time of harvest and joy, with “golden” evoking a sense 
of abundance, warmth, and prosperity. In this context, “golden-hued autumn of 2013” vividly 
captures the significance of the moment, symbolizing a period of fruitful cooperation and 
historical importance as President Xi Jinping unveiled the new vision.  
The spirit of cooperation was clearly demonstrated in the autumn of 2013 when President Xi 
Jinping visited Kazakhstan and Indonesia, introducing a new vision rooted in the legacy of the 
ancient Silk Road. 
8 
These initiatives, collectively known as the Belt and Road Initiative (BRI), would 
traverse deserts and oceans, span thousands of miles of rivers and mountains, and 
expand the grand tapestry woven by civilizations’ quest for progress over the 
millennia. (Para. 5) 
Meaning: These initiatives, collectively called the Belt and Road Initiative (BRI), would cross 
deserts and oceans, cover thousands of miles of rivers and mountains, and extend the rich 
tapestry woven by civilizations’ pursuit of progress over the centuries. 
Note: The phrase “grand tapestry” serves as a metaphor, representing the interconnected 
history and progress of civilizations. This metaphorical language elevates the description from 
physical infrastructure to a conceptual level of cultural exchange, suggesting that civilizations 
are intricately linked and their collective achievements form a beautiful, cohesive whole.  
9 
By weaving a tapestry of interconnectedness and mutual benefit, China envisions a 
world where collaboration and mutual understanding will unite the global 
community, creating a fairer, more equitable, and harmonious global environment, 
where humanity will thrive as one. (Para. 7) 
Meaning: Through fostering global connectivity and shared prosperity, China promotes a 
vision of international cooperation built on mutual understanding. This approach seeks to unify 
nations in creating a more just, balanced, and peaceful world order where all societies can 
flourish together. 
Note: The phrase “weaving a tapestry of interconnectedness and mutual benefit” is a metaphor. 
It compares global cooperation and shared benefits to the act of weaving a tapestry. This 
metaphor makes the abstract concept of international collaboration more tangible and vivid by 
likening it to the creation of a solid and intricate piece of art. 
 
 
Translation  
编织美好世界 
1 
热带清晨的金色阳光洒向万隆，为这座城市镀上希望的光芒。时值1955 年，世界
正因冷战的紧张局势而陷入分裂。然而在独立大厦宏伟的围墙内，来自 29 个亚非

13 
 
国家和地区的领导人高举团结的旗帜，齐聚一堂。不过，在希望的表象之下，空气
中却弥漫着紧张的气息。激烈的争论在暗中酝酿，分歧也在滋生，当反对共产主义
国家的声音开始高涨时，情况更是如此。 
2 
正是在这压力俱增的时刻，中国代表团挺身而出。周恩来总理以平静而坚定的姿态，
提出了“求同存异”的主张。他平和的话语化干戈为玉帛。随着会议的推进，周恩
来表示愿意在和平共处五项原则的基础上，同各国团结合作。在此基础上，会议提
出了处理国际关系的万隆会议十项原则，引起全球共鸣。在万隆会议上，新中国展
现出一个注重和平发展的国家形象，这一角色在随后的几十年里不断与时俱进。 
3   1971 年10 月25 日，第26 届联合国大会通过第2758 号决议，恢复中华人民共和
国在联合国的合法席位。大会堂内顿时掌声雷动。在联合国的宏大舞台上，中国外
交新时代拉开帷幕，展现出国际合作的勃勃生机。联合国这个汇聚众多国家的国际
组织，成为中国开展多领域、多边外交的重要场所。从另一个角度来看，中国在联
合国合法席位的恢复，让世界四分之一的人口拥有了国际话语权。至此，联合国在
规模和构成上才真正称得上是一个全球性的组织。 
4   50 年来，中国始终坚定不移地维护以联合国为核心的国际体系，不断维护与践行
真正的多边主义。金砖国家（BRICS）充分体现了中国参与全球治理、推动真正多
边合作的坚定决心。作为南南合作的典范，金砖国家为新兴市场国家和发展中国家
加强团结合作、维护共同利益提供了重要平台。作为金砖国家的创始成员国，中国
为金砖国家的发展做出了重要贡献。值得一提的是，在 2017 年担任金砖国家主席
国期间，中国提出了开创性的“金砖 +”合作模式，首次在全球范围内拓展伙伴关
系。2024 年1 月，金砖成员国的数量增至10 个。目前，约30 个国家正在叩响“金
砖之门”。金砖国家提高了发展中国家的声音，进一步推动构建更加公平、民主的
国际秩序。 
5   合作精神在2013 年金秋时节得以彰显。习近平主席在访问哈萨克斯坦和印度尼西
亚之际，勾勒出一个源于传奇丝绸之路的崭新愿景。他提出共建“丝绸之路经济带”
和 “21 世纪海上丝绸之路”的重大倡议。这些倡议统称为“一带一路”倡议，它
将穿越沙漠和海洋，跨越千山万水，拓展人类文明数千年追求进步所织就的宏大图
景。 
6   中国秉持“共商共建共享”原则，已同150 多个国家和30 多个国际组织签署了共
建“一带一路”合作文件，推动“一带一路”倡议蓬勃发展，使其成为深受欢迎的
国际公共产品和国际合作平台。中欧班列是其中亮点。它横贯亚欧大陆，通过三条
主要物流走廊连接中国城市与欧洲主要枢纽，沿途经停多个重要边境口岸。这条铁
路网络将货物运送至欧洲25 个国家的227 个城市以及亚洲11 个国家的100 多个城
市。截至2024 年11 月，中欧班列累计开行超过10 万列，运输货物超过1,100 万标

14 
 
准箱，总货值超过4,200 亿美元。这条不断壮大的“钢铁驼队”稳定地运送着各类
货物，从日常用品到电动汽车，极大地促进了亚欧之间陆路物流与贸易。 
7   中国外交历程顺应着国际形势的变化，彰显出对构建“人类命运共同体”的坚定承
诺。这一愿景与和平共处五项原则一脉相承，为这些经久不衰的理念注入了新活力，
并在如今通过“一带一路”倡议结出硕果。中国通过编织互联互通、互利共赢的网
络，擘画出一个全球愿景：在协作发展和相互理解中，全球社会紧密团结在一起，
打造一个更加公平、公正、和谐的世界，让人类作为一个共同体蓬勃发展。

15 
 
Intercultural communication skill  
Teaching suggestions 
The aim of these steps is to help Ss understand the concept and function of metaphors in 
communication, particularly their cultural dimensions, and to analyze how they convey 
complex ideas succinctly. Additionally, the focus is on enabling Ss to create effective original 
metaphors, thereby enhancing their creativity and cross-cultural communication skills. 
⚫ 
Define metaphors and explain their role in communication. 
⚫ 
Provide Ss with culturally diverse metaphors (e.g., China’s “tapestry of civilizations” vs. 
Western “melting pot”). Have them dissect the symbolism. 
⚫ 
Explain how metaphors simplify abstract ideas in the text (e.g., “global cooperation as a 
tapestry”). 
⚫ 
Guide Ss to create original metaphors. First, identify a complex concept (e.g., climate 
change) and list its core traits (e.g., interconnectedness, urgency). Second, brainstorm 
analogies (e.g., “domino effect,” “hourglass”) and vote on the most vivid option. Third, 
refine metaphors collaboratively (e.g., Climate change is a domino set in motion, and once 
it starts falling, it’s hard to stop the chain reaction that disrupts the balance of our natural 
world). 
* Remind Ss to access the video micro-lecture in the digital textbook to learn more about 
the skill on Ucampus. 
 
Sample answers 
⚫ “On the grand stage of the UN, the curtain rose on a new era of diplomacy for 
China, revealing a dynamic scene of international cooperation.” (Para. 3) 
The metaphors “grand stage of the UN” and “the curtain rose” frame diplomacy as a 
theatrical performance, symbolizing the UN as a platform for global engagement. It not 
only brings to life the idea of China stepping into a new diplomatic phase at the UN, but 
also evokes vivid imagery of drama and transformation, emphasizing China’s proactive 
role in the new era.  
⚫ “Currently, about 30 countries are knocking on the ‘BRICS door’.” (Para. 4) 
The metaphor of “knocking on the ‘BRICS door’” vividly illustrates the eagerness of 
countries to join the BRICS group. It simplifies the complex process of seeking 
membership into a relatable visual image, showing the group’s attractiveness and 
influence.

16 
 
⚫ “BRICS amplifies developing countries’ voices” (Para. 4) 
The metaphor uses “voices” to symbolize the opinions, perspectives, or positions of 
developing countries, and “amplifies” suggests strengthening or making these 
perspectives more impactful and noticeable. 
⚫ “this ever-growing ‘steel caravan’” (Para. 6) 
Referring to the China-Europe Railway Express as a “steel caravan” draws a connection 
between modern transportation infrastructure and historical trade routes. This metaphor 
emphasizes continuity and the enduring importance of trade connections across regions.

China’s Global Development Initiative, Global Security Initiative, and Global Civilization 
Initiative provide a feasible path and are a crucial cornerstone for building a global community 
of shared future. These initiatives uphold the spirit of openness and inclusiveness, aiming to 
address development challenges, tackle security issues, and promote mutual learning among

17 
 
civilizations. They demonstrate China’s theoretical innovation and practical contributions to 
global governance, reflect its steadfast commitment to promoting world peace and prosperity, 
and breathe greater certainty and hope into an uncertain world.  
 
Critical thinking  
Sample answers  
1 
Zheng He’s voyages built connections, fostered trade, and promoted harmony among 
nations. His spirit of peaceful exploration, mutual respect, and cultural exchange is deeply 
embedded in China’s modern diplomatic philosophy. One significant example is the Belt and 
Road Initiative (BRI), which aims to connect countries across Asia, Europe, and Africa through 
infrastructure, trade, and cultural exchange. This initiative reflects Zheng He’s legacy of 
creating mutually beneficial relationships and enhancing global connectivity. Additionally, 
China’s active participation in multilateral organizations like the United Nations and its crucial 
role in addressing global challenges, such as climate change and poverty alleviation, 
demonstrate its commitment to peaceful cooperation and shared progress, echoing Zheng He’s 
diplomatic ethos. 
 
2 
As a university student, I believe I can play a meaningful role in promoting mutual 
learning between civilizations and fostering cultural exchange. First, I can participate in 
international exchange programs or cultural events organized by my university. These 
opportunities allow me to interact with students from diverse backgrounds, share my culture, 
and learn about theirs, creating deeper connections and understanding. 
Second, I can use social media to share my culture with a global audience. By posting 
stories, photos, or videos about our cultural traditions, I can spark conversations with people 
from different parts of the world and learn about their cultures in return. 
Finally, I can actively seek collaboration with students from different cultural 
backgrounds on academic or community projects. Working together not only helps us exchange 
ideas and perspectives but also fosters mutual respect and understanding. 
Through these actions, I can contribute to building a more inclusive and harmonious 
global community, where civilizations learn from one another and grow together.

18 
 
Exploring the world  
Amid a fragile global economy, persistent security challenges, and ongoing conflicts 
between civilizations, China has proposed building a global community of shared future. With 
the Global Development Initiative, Global Security Initiative, and Global Civilization Initiative 
as action plans, China calls on nations around the world to unite, tackle global challenges, and 
foster peace, development, and mutual understanding to achieve sustainable prosperity. The 
Understanding China section highlights China’s efforts to build a global community of shared 
future, and the Exploring the World section examines how other countries share similar ideas 
with China and work toward building a better future together. The text highlights Africa’s 
“Agenda 2063” for common prosperity, the EU’s GDPR for universal security, and the 
Conference on Dialogue of Asian Civilizations for cultural exchange.  
T should focus on teaching the actions various countries have taken to promote prosperity, 
peace, and cultural exchange around the world. Ss should grasp how initiatives like “Agenda 
2063” and the GDPR contribute to regional and global stability and progress, and how cultural 
dialogues enrich mutual learning. The challenge lies in guiding Ss to deeply analyze the 
complexities of international cooperation and civilization exchanges. It involves understanding 
diverse cultural contexts and the long-term impacts of such collaborative efforts. 
The text offers an excellent opportunity for Ss to compare global initiatives aimed at 
creating a better future with China’s vision of building a global community of shared future, 
along with its corresponding actions. T should guide Ss to learn from the global efforts and 
share China’s perspectives and contributions, and inspire them to actively contribute to shaping 
a better world. 
 
Text: Moving forward: Cultivating a tomorrow together 
Notes 
Launched in 2018, the Single African Air Transport Market (SAATM) is a flagship project 
of the African Union’s “Agenda 2063.” It aims to liberalize the African aviation industry, 
transforming it into a single market by deregulating air services and opening regional air 
markets to transnational competition. The undertaking is expected to support the advancement 
of the continent’s socio-economic development and to increase intra-African air connectivity. 
The term Bedouin is used to describe a large group of people who originated in the Arabian 
Peninsula but have also spread throughout the Middle East and North Africa. The Bedouin 
people are culturally, linguistically, and religiously part of a wider Arab society. “Bedouin” 
has historically been used to refer to those who practiced the traditional nomadic pastoralist 
way of life, raising camels and other livestock for grazing. However, most Bedouin people live 
settled lives today, either on farms or in cities. 
* T can guide Ss to study the multimodal annotations in the digital textbook on Ucampus.

19 
 
Language points 
1 It is therefore imperative that countries act together to build a better world. (Para. 1) 
imperative: a. extremely important and needing to be done or dealt with immediately 紧急
的；极重要的 
Note: The word “imperative” is commonly used in the following structures: “It is imperative 
that ...” and “It is imperative (for sb.) to do sth.” For example:  
It is imperative for governments to combat climate change now, as delaying action could have 
serious consequences. 
This sentence serves as a call to action for global collaboration. 
2 … as nations join hands to share resources and tackle common challenges … (Para.  
2) 
join hands: work together with another organization, country, etc. to help and support each 
other （与另一个组织、国家等）联手，联合起来 
Nations should join hands to address severe global challenges and collaboratively build a 
harmonious and sustainable future. 
3 The General Data Protection Regulation (GDPR), which came into effect in 2018 after 
nearly four years’ discussions and a two-year transition period, stands as a testament 
to this effort. (Para. 7) 
stand as a testament to: serve as evidence or proof of sth. 作为…的证明 
The company’s growth over the years stands as a testament to its successful business strategies. 
Note: This sentence metaphorically frames the GDPR as a symbolic achievement. The “two-
year transition period” refers to the time from 2016, when the GDPR was officially adopted, to 
2018, when it came into effect, allowing EU member states and businesses sufficient time to 
adapt to and prepare for its requirements, ensuring smooth implementation. 
4 Its far-reaching impact has influenced global corporations, governments, and Internet 
users … (Para. 8) 
far-reaching: a. if you describe actions, events, or changes as far-reaching, you mean that they 
have a very great influence and affect a great number of things 影响深远的 
The invention of the Internet has had a far-reaching impact, transforming communication and 
commerce globally. 
5 This day serves as a reminder of the wealth of the world’s cultures … (Para. 12) 
reminder: n. [C] sth. that makes you notice, remember, or think about sth. 起提醒作用的东
西 
Note: Pay attention to the common collocations “reminder of” and “serve / act as a reminder.” 
For example:

20 
 
The ruined building was a constant reminder of the war. 
The cold served as a reminder that winter wasn’t over yet. 
6 Living in the same global village, humanity has increasingly emerged as a community 
in which everyone carries within themselves a little bit of others. (Para. 13) 
Note: The term “global village” is used as a metaphor for an interconnected world. The 
sentence suggests that as humanity becomes more interconnected, people increasingly embody 
aspects of others within themselves. This could refer to shared values, cultural influences, 
mutual understanding, or empathy that arise from living in a globally connected community. 
Essentially, it emphasizes the idea that no one exists in isolation, and everyone is shaped, at 
least in part, by their interactions and connections with others around the world. 
 
 
Translation 
携手前行：共创美好未来 
1   当今世界互联互通，且充满不确定性，我们的选择不仅深刻影响当前局势，还关乎
子孙后代的福祉。因此，各国必须携手共建更美好的世界。不过，这个更美好的世
界究竟意味着什么？我们又该如何实现这一愿景？ 
2   更美好的世界意味着全球各国携手合作、共同发展。各国齐心协力，共享资源，共
面挑战，为全球可持续发展铺平道路。 
3   以非洲为例，非洲联盟成员国正在《2063 年议程》框架下开展合作。这项2015 年
制定的发展蓝图旨在用50 年的时间建设一个繁荣、一体化的非洲大陆。这一倡议
呼吁非洲社会各领域采取集体行动，是南南合作的典范。 
4   实现《2063 年议程》的关键在于在基础设施、教育、科学、技术、艺术和文化等领
域开展旗舰项目。其中，单一非洲航空运输市场（SAATM）尤为引人注目。该项目
旨在通过航空运输自由化和监管流程标准化，促进非洲大陆内部的互联互通和经济
一体化发展。这一举措不仅促进了非洲内部的旅行往来，还降低成本，提升通达性，
推动了经济一体化进程。  
5   这些举措成效显著。在首个十年实施期结束后，2022 年和2023 年非洲国内生产总
值（GDP）增长率分别为4.1%和3.1%，高于同期全球3.2%和2.7%的平均预期增长
率。 
6   更美好的世界需要国际社会团结一致、共同提升环境安全。 
7   随着科技的飞速发展，网络安全正演变为日益复杂且互相关联的重大挑战。作为区
域一体化的典范，欧盟已采取措施，集合成员国的力量来应对这一挑战。其中，《通
用数据保护条例》（GDPR）在经过长达四年的深入讨论及两年的过渡期，于2018
年正式实施，成为欧盟应对网络安全挑战的有力证明。《通用数据保护条例》规定

21 
 
了个人数据处理和保护的规则和指南，不仅适用于欧盟全部27 个成员国，而且涵
盖了跨境数据处理的相关条款。 
8   为确保《通用数据保护条例》的顺利实施，各欧盟成员国监管机构通力协作，优化
流程，共享专业知识，强化协调联动。该条例被视为全球最严格的隐私和安全法规，
可对违规者处以高达数千万欧元的巨额罚款。其深远影响已延伸至全球范围内的企
业、政府机构和互联网用户，成为多国制定数据保护法规的重要参考。 
9   除《通用数据保护条例》外，过去几十年间还涌现了多项国际合作成果，如《布达
佩斯网络犯罪公约》《马拉博协议》等。这些公约通过促进信息共享、提升能力建
设，以及开展联合威胁评估，使国际社会更有效地应对不断变化的网络形势。 
10  更美好的世界在不同文化思想的交流与互鉴中蓬勃发展。 
11  亚洲为此提供了生动例证。从阿拉伯半岛贝都因人的游牧生活方式，到东南亚独特
的岛屿文化，再到中国深厚的儒家传统，亚洲孕育了古老而璀璨的文明。为加强对
话、深化合作，2019 年举办了以“亚洲文明交流互鉴与命运共同体”为主题的亚洲
文明对话大会。这一开放包容的对话平台举办了110 多项活动，包括嘉年华、文化
展览、美食节和艺术展览，彰显了亚洲文明的蓬勃魅力。在嘉年华期间，各国艺术
家同台献艺，精彩呈现了激昂的中国鼓、绚丽的印度舞蹈、悠扬的中亚民歌以及古
老的南亚传统表演。亚洲正通过其文明与艺术向世界传递着希望，展现出一个既深
植于自身悠久传统，同时又积极寻求相互学习与融合发展的区域形象。 
12  文明间的交流与对话已超越亚洲大陆。经中国倡议，联合国一致通过将每年6 月10
日设立为“国际文明对话日”。这个节日提醒人们，世界文化多姿多彩，跨文化对
话蕴含着丰富的内涵和强大的力量。 
13  人类生活在同一个地球村，逐渐形成一个你中有我、我中有你的共同体。只有各国
都致力于追求共同利益，和谐共处，合作共赢，才能携手共建一个开放包容、清洁
美丽、持久和平、全民安全、共同繁荣的世界。
